(\operatorname{ad}_{c}e_{i})^{-c_{ij}^{\mathfrak{q}}}e_{j},&j\neq i,\end{cases}\\
\\
T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\underline{L}_{j})&=L_{j}L_{i}^{-c_{ij}^{\mathfrak{q}}};&T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\underline{f}_{i})&=\begin{cases}K_{i}^{-1}e_{i},&j=i,\\
(\lambda_{ij}^{\mathfrak{q}})^{-1}(\operatorname{ad}_{c}f_{i})^{-c_{ij}^{\mathfrak{q}}}f_{j},&j\neq i,\end{cases}\end{aligned}

The underlined letters denote the generators of
U_{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}
.

Analogously,
e_{\beta_{k}}=T_{i_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}\dots T_{i_{k-1}}(e_{i_{k}})
and
f_{\beta_{k}}=T_{i_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}\dots T_{i_{k-1}}(f_{i_{k}})
belong to
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
and
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
, respectively and by
[
9
, Theorem 11]
the sets

(4.21)

\displaystyle\big\{e_{\beta_{1}}^{n_{1}}e_{\beta_{2}}^{n_{2}}\dots e_{\beta_{\ell}}^{n_{\ell}}:0\leq n_{i}<\widetilde{N}_{\beta_{i}}\big\}

and

\displaystyle\big\{f_{\beta_{1}}^{m_{1}}f_{\beta_{2}}^{m_{2}}\dots f_{\beta_{\ell}}^{m_{\ell}}:0\leq m_{j}<\widetilde{N}_{\beta_{j}}\big\}

are bases of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
and
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
, respectively. Thus the following set is a basis of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
:

(4.22)

\displaystyle\big\{e_{\beta_{1}}^{m_{1}}\dots e_{\beta_{\ell}}^{m_{\ell}}K_{1}^{a_{1}}\dots K_{\theta}^{a_{\theta}}L_{1}^{b_{1}}\dots L_{\theta}^{b_{\theta}}f_{\beta_{1}}^{n_{1}}\dots f_{\beta_{\ell}}^{n_{\ell}}:0\leq m_{j},\,n_{j}<\widetilde{N}_{\beta_{j}},\,a_{i},\,b_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\big\}.

4.5.
The central subalgebras
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

In this subsection and the next
\mathfrak{q}
does not need to be in a family, just
\dim{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{q}}<\infty
is assumed.
Set

(4.23)

\displaystyle\mathtt{N}=\operatorname{lcm}\{N_{\beta}:\beta\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}.

To start with, we consider a subalgebra
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
. Then
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
is generated by

(4.24)

\displaystyle e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},

\displaystyle f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},

\displaystyle K_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}},

\displaystyle L_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}},

\displaystyle\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+},

(4.25)

\displaystyle K_{\beta}^{\pm\mathtt{N}},

\displaystyle L_{\beta}^{\pm\mathtt{N}},

\displaystyle\beta\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+};

this is a normal
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
-graded Hopf subalgebra of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
[
9
, Proposition 21, Theorem 33]
, which may be different from the one in
[
9
, p. 18]

since we add the generators in (
4.25
) what actually only affects the types
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
,
see Proposition
4.8
.
These new generators are necessary for
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
to be finitely generated as
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
-module.

The following subalgebras of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
are also needed:

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}\langle e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}:\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\rangle,

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}\langle f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}:\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\rangle.

Notice that
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
coincides with the subalgebra introduced right after (
3.17
), see
[
9
, Theorem 29]
.
For
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
to be central in
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
we need the following condition that

we assume from now on:

(4.26)

\displaystyle q_{\alpha\beta}^{N_{\beta}}=1,

\displaystyle\alpha\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}},\;\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}.

Remark 4.4
.

(a)

If (
4.26
) holds, then
q_{\beta\alpha}^{N_{\beta}}=1

[
9
, Lemma 24]
.

(b)

Condition (
4.26
) is equivalent to the following one:

(4.27)

\displaystyle q_{\alpha_{i}\beta}^{N_{\beta}}=1,

\displaystyle\text{ for all }i\in\mathbb{I},

\displaystyle\;\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}.

The reduction to simple roots is clear. Since
q_{\alpha\beta}^{N_{\beta}}=q_{\alpha\underline{\beta}}

and
\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
is a basis of the root system
\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}
, the reduction from
\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
to
\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
holds.

(c)

Let
i\in\mathbb{I}
.
Condition (
4.26
) holds for
\mathfrak{q}
if and only if it holds for

\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})
.

Indeed,
\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})_{\alpha\beta}=\mathfrak{q}_{s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\alpha)s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta)}
for all
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{Z}^{\theta}
by (
3.11
),
and by
[
5
, Lemma 2.3]
we have
s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}})=\mathfrak{O}^{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}
,
N^{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}_{s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta)}=N^{\mathfrak{q}}_{\beta}
for all
\beta
.

When
\mathfrak{q}
is symmetric, we can quotient the large quantum by a central group subalgebra to remove the extra Cartan generators as in quantum groups.
However the condition of
\mathfrak{q}
being symmetric is not always compatible with (
4.26
) as we see next.

Example 4.5
.

Assume that
\mathfrak{q}
has Dynkin diagram
\textstyle{\overset{-1}{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{\overset{-1}{\circ}}
,
\xi\in\mathbb{G}_{N}^{\prime}
,
N>2
: it is of super type
{\mathbf{A}}(1|0)
. In this case,

\displaystyle\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}\}.

Condition (
4.27
) becomes

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=(q_{11}q_{12})^{N}=(-q_{12})^{N},

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=(q_{21}q_{22})^{N}=(-q_{21})^{N}

\displaystyle\iff q_{12}^{N}=(-1)^{N}=q_{21}^{N}.

We have two possibilities: if
N
is even, then
q_{12}=\xi^{k}
for some
k\in\mathbb{I}_{N}
, so
q_{21}=\xi^{1-k}
, and
\mathfrak{q}
is not symmetric.
If
N
is odd, then
q_{12}=-\xi^{k}
for some
k\in\mathbb{I}_{N}
, so
q_{21}=-\xi^{1-k}
. In this case
\mathfrak{q}
is symmetric only when
k=\frac{N+1}{2}
.

We consider also the Hopf subalgebras

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}\langle\{K_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}}:\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}\cup\{K_{\beta}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}:\beta\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}\rangle,

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}

\displaystyle=Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+0},

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}\langle\{L_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}}:\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}\cup\{L_{\beta}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}:\beta\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}\rangle,

\displaystyle\ Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

\displaystyle=Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-0}.

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}

\displaystyle=Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+0}Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-0}.

Remark 4.6
.

The following properties hold:

(a)

[
9
, Th. 23]
.
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
is a polynomial ring in variables
e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}
, respectively
f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}
,
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
.

(b)

The multiplication gives linear isomorphisms

Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\otimes Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+0}\otimes Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-0}\otimes Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\simeq Z_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\otimes Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
.

(c)

Recall the skew-derivations
\partial^{\mathfrak{q}}_{i}
,
\partial^{\mathfrak{q}^{(-1)}}_{i}
of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
, cf. (
4.14
). By
[
9
, Theorem 31]
,

(4.28)

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{i\in\mathbb{I}}\ker\partial_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{i\in\mathbb{I}}\ker\partial^{\mathfrak{q}^{(-1)}}_{i}.

(d)

The algebras
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}

are module finite over their central subalgebras
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
; just consider the PBW-bases in §
4.4
.

4.6.
Action of the Weyl groupoid on
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}

Next we prove invariance of the central Hopf subalgebras
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
under the Lusztig isomorphisms
T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}:U_{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}\to U_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
cf. §
4.4
.

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
i\in\mathbb{I}
. Then

T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}
restricts to an algebra isomorphism
T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}:Z_{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}\to Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Proof.

By (
4.28
),
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
(defined in terms of the root vectors which
depend on the expression of
\omega_{0}^{\mathfrak{q}}
)
is indeed indedependent of such expression; in particular we may choose
\omega_{0}^{\mathfrak{q}}=\sigma_{i_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}\dots\sigma_{i_{\ell}}
such that
i_{1}=i
. For simplicity we set
\mathfrak{p}=\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})
. As
\sigma_{i_{2}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\sigma_{i_{\ell}}
is reduced, we may extend it to a reduced expression of
\omega^{\mathfrak{p}}

[
32
, Corollary 3]
:

\displaystyle\omega_{0}^{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{i_{2}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\sigma_{i_{\ell}}\sigma_{j}

for some

\displaystyle j\in\mathbb{I}.

We set
\beta_{k}^{\prime}=\sigma_{i_{1}}(\beta_{k})=\sigma_{i_{2}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\sigma_{i_{k-1}}(\alpha_{i_{k}})
,
k\in\mathbb{I}_{2,\ell}
.
Hence

\displaystyle\{\beta_{k}^{\prime}:k\in\mathbb{I}_{2,\ell}\}

\displaystyle=s_{i}^{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}-\{\alpha_{i}\}\right)=\varDelta^{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}-\{\alpha_{i}\}.

As
\sigma_{i_{2}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\sigma_{i_{\ell}}(\alpha_{j})\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}
,
\sigma_{i_{2}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\sigma_{i_{\ell}}(\alpha_{j})\neq\beta_{k}^{\prime}
for
k\in\mathbb{I}_{2,\ell}
,
we have that
\sigma_{i_{2}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\sigma_{i_{\ell}}(\alpha_{j})=\alpha_{i}
. Then
\{N_{\beta^{\prime}}:\beta^{\prime}\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}\}=\{N_{\beta}:\beta\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}
, so

\operatorname{lcm}\{N_{\beta^{\prime}}:\beta^{\prime}\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}\}=\mathtt{N}
, and

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big(K_{i}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}\big)

\displaystyle=K_{i}^{\mp\mathtt{N}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big(K_{\beta_{k}^{\prime}}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}\big)

\displaystyle=K_{s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta_{k}^{\prime})}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}=K_{\beta_{k}}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle k>1,

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big(K_{i}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}\big)

\displaystyle=K_{i}^{\mp\mathtt{N}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big(K_{\beta_{k}^{\prime}}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}\big)

\displaystyle=K_{s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta_{k}^{\prime})}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}=K_{\beta_{k}}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle k>1.

Let
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}
. If
\beta=\beta_{k}^{\prime}
for some
k\in\mathbb{I}_{2,\ell}
, then

s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta_{k}^{\prime})=\beta_{k}
and
N_{\beta_{k}^{\prime}}=N_{\beta_{k}}
, hence

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big(K_{\beta_{k}^{\prime}}^{\pm N_{\beta_{k}^{\prime}}}\big)

\displaystyle=K_{s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta_{k}^{\prime})}^{\pm N_{\beta_{k}^{\prime}}}=K_{\beta_{k}}^{\pm N_{\beta_{k}}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big(e_{\beta_{k}^{\prime}}^{N_{\beta_{k}^{\prime}}}\big)

\displaystyle=T_{i_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}T_{i_{2}}\dots T_{i_{k-1}}(e_{i_{k}}^{N_{\beta_{k}}})=e_{\beta_{k}}^{N_{\beta_{k}}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}.

Otherwise
\beta=\alpha_{i}
, so
i
is a Cartan vertex and

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(K_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}})

\displaystyle=K_{i}^{\mp N_{\alpha_{i}}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})

\displaystyle=T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(e_{i}^{N_{\alpha_{i}}})=(f_{i}L_{i})^{N_{\alpha_{i}}}=q_{ii}^{\binom{N_{\alpha_{i}}}{2}}f_{i}^{N_{\alpha_{i}}}L_{i}^{N_{\alpha_{i}}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}.

Analogously,
T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(L_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}}),T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
for all
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}_{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}
, so
T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(Z_{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})})\subseteq Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Applying
T_{i}^{\mathfrak{p}}
we get the opposite inclusion.
∎

Let
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}
be the subgroup of
\Gamma
generated by
K_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}}
,
L_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}}
,
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
and let
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}
be the subgroup generated by
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
K_{\beta}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}
,
L_{\beta}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}
,
\beta\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
: we have that
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}=\mathbb{C}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Next we check that
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}
for
\mathfrak{q}
as in the Appendix, not of type super
A
, so the subalgebra
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
is generated by (
4.24
) and coincides with the one in
[
9
, p. 18]
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Assume that
\mathfrak{q}
belongs to one of the families in the Appendix
A
.

(a)

If
\mathfrak{q}
is not of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
,
k\in\mathbb{I}_{\lfloor\frac{\theta+1}{2}\rfloor}
, then
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}
is generated by (
4.24
).

(b)

Let
\mathfrak{q}
be of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
,
k\in\mathbb{I}_{\lfloor\frac{\theta+1}{2}\rfloor}
,
\eta\in\varDelta_{+}^{\mathfrak{q}}-\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
. Then

\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\langle K_{\eta}^{\mathtt{N}}\rangle\times\langle L_{\eta}^{\mathtt{N}}\rangle
, and

Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}
is generated by
\{K_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}},L_{\beta}^{\pm N_{\beta}}:\beta\in\varDelta^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}

and
K_{\eta}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}
,
L_{\eta}^{\pm\mathtt{N}}
.

Proof.

First we notice that
T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}
restricts to group isomorphisms
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq\Lambda_{\rho_{i}\mathfrak{q}}
,
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}\simeq\Lambda_{\rho_{i}\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}
since the restrictions
(T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}})_{|U_{\rho_{i}\mathfrak{q}}^{+0}}:U_{\rho_{i}\mathfrak{q}}^{+0}\to U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+0}
and
(T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}})_{|U_{\rho_{i}\mathfrak{q}}^{-0}}:U_{\rho_{i}\mathfrak{q}}^{-0}\to U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-0}
are given by
s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}
. Hence it is enough to consider one matrix for each Weyl equivalence class.

Assume that
\mathfrak{q}
is as in the Appendix
A
and is not of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
. If
\mathfrak{q}
is
of Cartan type, then
\varDelta_{+}^{\mathfrak{q}}=\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
, hence
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
For the other types we check the equality
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}
case-by-case when the Dynkin diagram is the one in Tables
2
and
3
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}
be of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
with Dynkin diagram in Table
2
,
\eta\in\varDelta_{+}^{\mathfrak{q}}-\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
. Then
\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}=\{\alpha_{ij}|i\leq j<k\text{ or }k<i\leq j\}
,
N_{\beta}=N
for all
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
,

\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}=\langle K_{i}^{N},L_{i}^{N}|i\neq k\rangle
,
\eta=\alpha_{ij}
for some
i\leq k\leq j
and

\mathtt{N}=N
if
N
is even while
\mathtt{N}=2N
if
N
is odd. Hence
K_{k}^{\mathtt{N}}
,
L_{k}^{\mathtt{N}}
belong to the subgroup generated by
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
K_{\eta}^{\mathtt{N}}
and
L_{\eta}^{\mathtt{N}}
.
On the other hand,
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{q}}
is generated by
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
K_{k}^{\mathtt{N}}
and
L_{k}^{\mathtt{N}}
, thus the statement follows.
∎

5.
The specialization setting for large quantum groups

In this section we introduce the non-restricted integral form of
U_{\mathbf{q}}
and prove that the
large quantum group
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a specialization of it. We also introduce restricted integral forms of the subalgebras
U_{\mathbf{q}}^{\pm}

and establish pairing results for the corresponding specializations. The latter integral forms will play a key role
in our treatment of Poisson order structures on the large quantum groups
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
and their Borel and unipotent subalgebras.

5.1.
Integral forms

In order to implement the ideas of Section
2
,
we need to consider forms over suitable rings, generalizing
[
20
]
. For simplicity, we set

(5.1)

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1},(\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{s}\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}-1)^{-1}:i\neq j\in\mathbb{I},0\leq s<-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}]\subset\mathbb{C}(\nu).

By (3.9),
\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{s}\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}\neq 0
for
0\leq s<-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}
.

We now define the
(non-restricted) integral forms
as the
\mathsf{A}
-subalgebras

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{A}\langle E_{i}:i\in\mathbb{I}\rangle\subset U_{\mathbf{q}}^{+},

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{A}[K_{i}^{\pm 1},L_{i}^{\pm 1}:i\in\mathbb{I}]\subset U_{\mathbf{q}}^{0},

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{-}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{A}\langle F_{i}:i\in\mathbb{I}\rangle\subset U_{\mathbf{q}}^{-},

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{A}\langle K_{i}^{\pm 1},L_{i}^{\pm 1},E_{i},F_{i}:i\in\mathbb{I}\rangle\subset U_{\mathbf{q}},

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\geqslant}

\displaystyle=U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}\otimes_{\mathsf{A}}\mathsf{A}[K_{i}^{\pm 1}:i\in\mathbb{I}],

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\leqslant}

\displaystyle=U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{-}\otimes_{\mathsf{A}}\mathsf{A}[L_{i}^{\pm 1}:i\in\mathbb{I}].

These are crucial for our purposes. We have again a triangular decomposition

(5.2)

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}\otimes_{\mathsf{A}}U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{0}\otimes_{\mathsf{A}}U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{-}

\displaystyle\simeq U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}.

The surjectivity
of this multiplication map follows from the cross relations (
4.4
),
(
4.5
) and (
4.6
), while the injectivity follows from (
4.8
). Recall (
4.15
) for the next result.

Lemma 5.1
.

For all
i\neq j
in
\mathbb{I}
,
(\lambda_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}})^{-1}\in\mathsf{A}
.

Proof.

If
\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}=1
, then using that
\mathbf{q}_{ii}\in\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1}]^{\times}
we have

\displaystyle(\lambda_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}}q_{ii}^{c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}(c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}-1)}(\mathbf{q}_{ii}-1)^{-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}}\prod_{0\leq s<-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}}(\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{s}\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}-1)^{-2}\in\mathsf{A}

Otherwise
\mathbf{q}_{ii}
is a root of unity of order
1-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}
, so because
(-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}})_{\mathbf{q}_{ii}}^{!}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, we have

\displaystyle(\lambda_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji})^{-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}}}{(-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}})_{\mathbf{q}_{ii}}^{!}}\prod_{0\leq s<-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}}(\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{s}\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}-1)^{-1}\in\mathsf{A}.\qed

Example 5.2
.

Let
\mathbf{q}
be of modular type
\mathtt{br}(2)
, respectively
\mathtt{wk}(4)
, see §
A.3
.
Then
\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1},(\nu-1)^{-1},(\nu-\zeta)^{-1}]
, respectively
\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1},(\nu-1)^{-1},(\nu+1)^{-1}]
.

Recall the Hopf skew-pairing from §
4.2
. We now define the
restricted integral forms
,
that also play a central role in this paper, as the
\mathsf{A}
-submodules

(5.3)

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}

\displaystyle:=\{y\in U_{\mathbf{q}}^{-}|\langle y,U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}\rangle\subset\mathsf{A}\},

\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}+}

\displaystyle:=\{x\in U_{\mathbf{q}}^{+}|\langle U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{-},x\rangle\subset\mathsf{A}\}.

Indeed, these are
\mathsf{A}
-subalgebras of
U_{\mathbf{q}}^{-}
and
U_{\mathbf{q}}^{+}
, respectively. This
follows from the fact that
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\pm}
are braided Hopf subalgebras of
U_{\mathbf{q}}^{\pm}
over
\mathsf{A}
and the properties of Hopf skew-pairings.

5.2.
PBW-bases of integral forms

Recall the Lusztig isomorphisms
T_{i}^{\mathbf{q}}
from §
4.4
.

Lemma 5.3
.

(a)

T_{i}^{\mathbf{q}}
restricts to an
\mathsf{A}
-algebra isomorphism
T_{i}^{\mathbf{q}}:U_{\rho_{i}(\mathbf{q}),\mathsf{A}}\to U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}
,
i\in\mathbb{I}
.

(b)

Let
\beta\in\varDelta_{+}
. Then
E_{\beta},F_{\beta}\in U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}
.

Proof.

(a)
follows from (
4.20
)
and Lemma
5.1
, while
(b)
from

(a)
and (
4.17
).
∎

Proposition 5.4
.

The sets (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) are
\mathsf{A}
-bases of
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\pm}
and
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}
, respectively.

Proof.

We consider the case of
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}
, the other being analogous. Let
Y
be the set of
PBW monomials of
U_{\mathbf{q}}^{+}
from (
4.18
).
By Lemma
5.3
,
Y\subset U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}
.
The defining relations of
U_{\mathbf{q}}^{+}
involve products of
E_{i}
with coefficients in
\mathsf{A}
, hence we may prove recursively that, for
j>k
,
E_{\beta_{j}}E_{\beta_{k}}\in\mathsf{A}Y
, the
\mathsf{A}
-module generated by
Y
, where each monomial in the expansion has letters
E_{\beta_{t}}
,
j>t>k
;
see the proof of
[
33
, Theorem 4.8]
. Thus
\mathsf{A}Y
is a left ideal containing
1
, so
\mathsf{A}Y=U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}
.
This fact and the direct sum decomposition
U_{\mathbf{q}}^{+}=\oplus_{y\in Y}\mathbb{C}(\nu)y
imply that

U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}=\oplus_{y\in Y}\mathsf{A}y
.
∎

Recall the notation
\widetilde{N}_{\beta}
in §
3.7
.
Next we consider the quantum divided powers

\displaystyle F_{\beta_{j}}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\frac{F_{\beta_{j}}^{n}}{(n)_{\mathbf{q}_{\beta_{j}\beta_{j}}}^{!}},

\displaystyle E_{\beta_{j}}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\frac{E_{\beta_{j}}^{n}}{(n)_{\mathbf{q}_{\beta_{j}\beta_{j}}}^{!}},

\displaystyle 0\leq n<\widetilde{N}_{\beta_{j}}.

Proposition 5.5
.

For
j\in\mathbb{I}_{\ell}
, let
n_{j},m_{j}
be such that

0\leq n_{j},m_{j}<\widetilde{N}_{\beta_{j}}
. Then

\displaystyle\langle F_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\dots F_{\beta_{\ell}}^{(n_{\ell})},E_{\beta_{1}}^{m_{1}}\dots E_{\beta_{\ell}}^{m_{\ell}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\delta_{n_{1}m_{1}}\ldots\delta_{m_{\ell}n_{\ell}}.

Proof.

Let
\eta_{j}=\langle F_{\beta_{j}},E_{\beta_{j}}\rangle
,
j\in\mathbb{I}_{\ell}
.
The same proof as
[
10
, Proposition 4.6]
shows that

\langle F_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\dots F_{\beta_{\ell}}^{(n_{\ell})},E_{\beta_{1}}^{m_{1}}\dots E_{\beta_{\ell}}^{m_{\ell}}\rangle=\delta_{n_{1}m_{1}}\ldots\delta_{m_{\ell}n_{\ell}}\eta_{1}^{n_{1}}\cdots\eta_{\ell}^{n_{\ell}},

As in
[
10
, 4.7]
, we see that
\eta_{j}=1
:
here
\langle F_{i},E_{i}\rangle=1
, there
\langle F_{i},E_{i}\rangle=-1
for
i\in\mathbb{I}
.
∎

Propositions
5.4
and
5.5

imply the following:

Corollary 5.6
.

The following sets are
\mathsf{A}
-basis of
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}
and
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}+}
, respectively:

(5.4)

\displaystyle\left\{F_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\dots F_{\beta_{\ell}}^{(n_{\ell})}:0\leq n_{j}<\widetilde{N}_{\beta_{j}}\right\}

and

\displaystyle\left\{E_{\beta_{1}}^{(m_{1})}\dots E_{\beta_{\ell}}^{(m_{\ell})}:0\leq m_{j}<\widetilde{N}_{\beta_{j}}\right\}.

5.3.
The specialization of
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}

As explained in Property
(c)
of the Appendix, there exists
\xi\in\mathbb{G}_{\infty}^{\prime}
such that

\mathbf{q}(\xi)=\mathfrak{q}
; we fix one such
\xi
.

We consider the setting in Section
2
assuming
R=\mathsf{A}
,
h=\nu-\xi
and the
R
-algebra
A
being either
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}
or its subalgebras
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\pm}
.
We claim that the map
\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1}]\to\mathbb{C}
,
\nu\mapsto\xi
extends to an isomorphism

\mathsf{A}/(\nu-\xi)\simeq\mathbb{C}
. For, if

\displaystyle\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{s}\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}-1

\displaystyle\mapsto q_{ii}^{s}q_{ij}q_{ji}-1=0

\displaystyle\text{for some }i\neq j,\;0\leq s<-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}},

then
0\leq-c_{ij}^{\mathfrak{q}}\leq s<-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}
, which contradicts Property
(c)
of the Appendix
A
.
Here and below we will use the bar notation
x\mapsto\overline{x}
for specializations.

Theorem 5.7
.

There are Hopf algebra (respectively, braided Hopf algebra) isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}:U_{\mathfrak{q}}\to U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}/(\nu-\xi)

and

\displaystyle\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}|_{U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}}:U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}\to U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\pm}/(\nu-\xi)

given by
e_{i}\mapsto\overline{E}_{i},f_{i}\mapsto\overline{F}_{i},K_{i}^{\pm 1}\mapsto\overline{K}_{i}^{\,\pm 1},L_{i}^{\pm 1}\mapsto\overline{L}_{i}^{\,\pm 1}

for all
i\in\mathbb{I}
. For each
i\in\mathbb{I}
, the following diagram is commutative:

(5.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.32555pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.32555pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}/(\nu-\xi).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

Proof.

The defining relations of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
hold in
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}/(\nu-\xi)
by the definition of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
in

[
9
]
and the presentation of
U_{\mathbf{q}}
in
[
8
]
.
Therefore, the map
\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
as above is well-defined. Moreover
\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
is surjective, since

\overline{E}_{i}
,
\overline{F}_{i}
,
\overline{K}_{i}^{\,\pm 1}
,
\overline{L}_{i}^{\,\pm 1}
generate
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}/(\nu-\xi)
as
\mathbb{C}
-algebra.

Now we check that (
5.5
) is a commutative diagram. Indeed, since Property
(c)
in the Appendix
A
holds and
\mathbf{q}\mapsto\mathfrak{q}
under the evaluation map, we have that

\displaystyle\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}\circ T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(e_{j})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{ad}_{c}\overline{E}_{i})^{-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}}\overline{E}_{j}=T_{i}^{\mathbf{q}}\circ\Xi_{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}(e_{j}),

\displaystyle\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}\circ T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(f_{j})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{ad}_{c}\overline{F}_{i})^{-c_{ij}^{\mathbf{q}}}\overline{F}_{j}=T_{i}^{\mathbf{q}}\circ\Xi_{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}(f_{j})

for
j\neq i
. Since
\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}\circ T_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(X)=T_{i}^{\mathbf{q}}\circ\Xi_{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}(X)
for
X\in\{e_{i},f_{i},K_{j}^{\pm 1},L_{j}^{\pm 1}\}
, the claim follows.
By (
5.5
),
\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}(E_{\beta})=\overline{E}_{\beta}
and
\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}(F_{\beta})=\overline{F}_{\beta}

for all
\beta\in\varDelta_{+}
. Hence
\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
sends the PBW basis of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
to that of
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}/(\nu-\xi)
, so
\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
, and its restrictions to
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
, are isomorphisms.
Clearly
\Xi_{\mathfrak{q}}
(and its restrictions) are isomorphisms of (braided) Hopf algebras.
∎

5.4.
The specialization of
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}\pm}

Recall the Lusztig algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}
§
3.9
and the identification of

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
with
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
as in §
4.3
.
For
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, define
\eta_{\beta}^{(n)}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}
such that

(5.6)

\displaystyle(\eta_{\beta_{j}}^{(n)},e_{\beta_{1}}^{m_{1}}\dots e_{\beta_{\ell}}^{m_{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&m_{j}=n,\,m_{k}=0\text{ for }k\neq j,\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

By
[
3
, Proposition 4.6]
, the set

\displaystyle\{\eta_{\beta_{1}}^{(n_{1})}\cdots\eta_{\beta_{\ell}}^{(n_{\ell})}:0\leq n_{j}<\widetilde{N}_{\beta_{j}}\}

is a basis of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and the algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}
is generated by

\displaystyle\{\eta_{\alpha_{i}}:i\in\mathbb{I}\}\cup\{\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})}:\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}\}.

The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
from (
3.15
) has a
\mathbb{C}
-basis

\{\iota^{*}(\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})}):\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}\}
and set of simple root vectors
\{\iota^{*}(\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})}):\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}\}
.
Similar results hold for the Lusztig algebra
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}^{(-1)}}
associated to
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{q}^{(-)}}\simeq U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
. The
corresponding elements of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}^{(-1)}}
, defined as in (
5.6
) using
f_{\beta}^{m}
instead of
e_{\beta}^{m}
,
will be denoted by
\theta_{\beta}^{(n)}
, where

\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Remark 5.8
.

The Lie algebras associated to
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}^{(-1)}}
as in Remark
3.7
are isomorphic to each
other, see the list in the Appendix
A
. Hence we have a Lie algebra isomorphism

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{(-1)}}^{-}\simeq\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}

where
\iota^{*}(\theta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})})\in\mathfrak{n}_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{(-1)}}^{+}
,
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
are mapped to the simple root vectors of
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
.

For a braided Hopf algebra
B
denote the braided opposite algebra
B^{\underline{\operatorname{op}}}
with product

\mu^{\operatorname{op}}:=\mu c^{-1}
where
\mu:B\times B\to B
is the product in
B
.

Proposition 5.9
.

There are
\mathbb{C}
-algebra anti-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \phi^{-}:U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}/(\nu-\xi)&\to\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}},\\
\phi^{+}:\Big(U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}+}/(\nu-\xi)\Big)^{\underline{\operatorname{op}}}&\to\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}^{(-1)}},\end{aligned}

given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \overline{F_{\beta}^{(n)}}&\mapsto\eta_{\beta}^{(n)},\\
\overline{E_{\beta}^{(n)}}&\mapsto\theta_{\beta}^{(n)},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}},\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Proof.

We prove the statement in the minus case, the plus case is analogous.
By Proposition
5.5
the Hopf skew-pairing

\langle\,,\,\rangle:U_{\mathbf{q}}^{-}\times U_{\mathbf{q}}^{+}\to\mathbb{C}(\nu)

restricts to a perfect
pairing

\displaystyle\langle\,,\,\rangle:U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}\times U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}\to\mathsf{A}.

Since
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}/(\nu-\xi)\simeq U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
as braided Hopf algebras, the latter pairing induces a non-degenerate pairing

\langle\,,\,\rangle:\big(U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}/(\nu-\xi)\big)\times U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\to\mathbb{C}
such that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\langle yy^{\prime},x\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle y\otimes y^{\prime},\Delta(x)\rangle,

\displaystyle y,y^{\prime}\in U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}/(\nu-\xi),x\in U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}

and we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}\times U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big(U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}/(\nu-\xi)\big)\times U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}}

By the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, we have a canonical vector space isomorphism

\displaystyle\phi^{-}:U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}/(\nu-\xi)\to\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}

such that

\displaystyle\langle Y,x\rangle

\displaystyle=(\phi^{-}(Y),x)

for all

Y\in U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}/(\nu-\xi),x\in U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
.
Comparing (
3.17
) and (
5.8
), we see that
\phi^{-}
is an algebra anti-isomorphism.
Using again Proposition
5.5
and the definition (
5.6
) of
\eta_{\beta}^{(n)}
, we get that
\phi^{-}
is given by

\overline{F_{\beta}^{(n)}}\mapsto\eta_{\beta}^{(n)}
for
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
∎

6.
Poisson orders on large quantum groups

By Theorem
5.7
, the large quantum group
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
fits in the context of
Section
2
and consequently the pair
(U_{\mathfrak{q}},\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}))
inherits a structure of Poisson order from deformation theory.
However the Poisson algebra
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}})
is often singular.
We prove that the central Hopf subalgebra
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
introduced in §
4.5
(which is of course regular)
is a Poisson subalgebra of
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}})
of the same dimension.
Thus
(U_{\mathfrak{q}},Z_{\mathfrak{q}})
has a structure of Poisson order that
restricts to the corresponding large quantum Borel and unipotent algebras.

6.1.
Poisson structure on
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}

We show that
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}

are Poisson subalgebras of
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}})
, respectively

\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})
,
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})
,
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+})
and
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-})
.

The coefficients of Poisson brackets that we use will be expressed in terms of a square matrix
{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}\in\mathbb{C}^{\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}\times\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}}
.
Furthermore, in the next section we will show that the Cartan matrix of the semisimple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
is also expressed in terms of the
entries of this matrix. The matrix
{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}
is defined as follows.
Let
\beta,\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
. As
q_{\beta\gamma}=\mathbf{q}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi)
, (
4.26
) implies
that there exists
\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\nu)\in\mathsf{A}
such that

(6.1)

\displaystyle 1-\mathbf{q}_{\beta\gamma}^{N_{\beta}N_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=(\nu-\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\nu).

Recall the notation
\underline{\beta}
from (
3.14
) and
the set
\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}
from Theorem
3.5
.
Define

(6.2)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}:=(\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi))_{\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}}.

We distinguish two cases, namely whether
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
or not. In the first case
we need one more generator to have finite type of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
as
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
-module, and
a fortiori

as
Z(U_{\mathfrak{q}})
-module.

Case 1
.

\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
with Dynkin diagram as in Table
2
. Set

(6.3)

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle:=\sum_{1\leq i\leq k}\alpha_{i}+\sum_{k<i\leq\theta}(i-k+1)\alpha_{i},

\displaystyle\underline{\eta}

\displaystyle:=N_{\eta}\eta,

recall (
3.12
). It is easy to see that
N_{\eta}=\mathtt{N}
, recall (
4.23
). Denote

(6.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}:=\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}\sqcup\{\underline{\eta}\}=\{\underline{\alpha}_{i}|i\neq k\}\sqcup\{\underline{\eta}\}.

We have the following:

(I)

By Proposition
4.8
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
is generated by (
4.24
) and
K_{\eta}^{\pm N_{\eta}}
,
L_{\eta}^{\pm N_{\eta}}
.

(II)

By direct computation,
q_{\underline{\alpha}_{i}\underline{\eta}}=q_{\underline{\eta}\underline{\alpha}_{i}}=1
. Arguing as in (
6.1
), there exist
\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\eta}(\nu)
,
\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\eta\alpha_{i}}(\nu)\in\mathsf{A}
such that

\displaystyle 1-\mathbf{q}_{\underline{\alpha}_{i}\underline{\eta}}

\displaystyle=(\nu-\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\eta}(\nu),

\displaystyle 1-\mathbf{q}_{\underline{\eta}\underline{\alpha}_{i}}

\displaystyle=(\nu-\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\eta\alpha_{i}}(\nu).

We check that the following equality holds:

(6.5)

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\eta}(\xi)+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\eta\alpha_{i}}(\xi)=0,

\displaystyle i\neq k.

(III)

Similarly,
q_{\underline{\eta}\underline{\eta}}=1
, so there exist
\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\eta\eta}(\nu)\in\mathsf{A}
such that

1-\mathbf{q}_{\underline{\eta}\underline{\eta}}=(\nu-\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\eta\eta}(\nu)
.
By direct computation,

(6.6)

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\eta\eta}(\xi)

\displaystyle=-N_{\eta}^{2}\xi\left(\theta-k+(\theta-k+1)^{2}\right)\neq 0.

Case 2
.

\mathfrak{q}
is not of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
. Set

(6.7)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}:=\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}.

It follows from Proposition
4.8
that

(6.8)

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+0}=\mathbb{C}[K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}:\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}_{\mathfrak{q}}],

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-0}=\mathbb{C}[L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}:\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}_{\mathfrak{q}}].

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
i\in\mathbb{I}
. Then
{\mathscr{P}}^{\rho_{i}(\mathbf{q})}={\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}
.

Proof.

First,
\varPi^{\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{q})}=s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}})
. Thus
\wp^{\rho_{i}(\mathbf{q})}_{s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta)s_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\gamma)}(\nu)=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\nu)
for
\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
by (
3.11
).
∎

The next theorem is the main result of this section.

Theorem 6.2
.

There are structures of Poisson order on the pairs

(6.9)

\displaystyle(U_{\mathfrak{q}},Z_{\mathfrak{q}}),

\displaystyle(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant},Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}),

\displaystyle(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant},Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}),

\displaystyle(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+},Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+})

and

\displaystyle(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-},Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-})

arising by restriction from the Poisson order on the corresponding algebra and its center
with Poisson bracket (
2.2
).
The central algebras
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
are Poisson-Hopf while

Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
are coideal Poisson subalgebras over the former.

In each of the pairs in (
6.9
) the second algebra is central to the first.
Using PBW bases one easily shows that in each case the first algebra is a finitely generated module over the second one
in the pair; here the introduction of the generators (
4.25
) for super type A is essential.
Because of Theorem
5.7
and Proposition
2.5
we are reduced to prove:

Proposition 6.3
.

The subalgebras
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}

are Poisson subalgebras of
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm})
,
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})
,
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})
and

\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}})
, respectively, under the Poisson bracket (
2.2
).

Observe that
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

are Poisson subalgebras of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Proof.

We apply Theorem
2.4
to the algebra
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{+}
, the automorphisms
\varsigma_{i}^{\mathbf{q}}
and the
(\operatorname{id},\varsigma_{i}^{\mathbf{q}})
-derivations

\partial_{i}^{\mathbf{q}}
,
i\in\mathbb{I}
to conclude that
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
defined as in (
2.5
)
is a Poisson subalgebra of
\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+})
. Now we have that

\displaystyle\overline{\varsigma}_{i}^{\;\mathbf{q}}

\displaystyle\overset{\star}{=}\varsigma_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{i}^{\;\mathbf{q}}

\displaystyle\overset{\ast}{=}\partial_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}

\displaystyle\subset\cap_{i\in\mathbb{I}}\ker(\varsigma_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}-\operatorname{id}).

The equality
\star
holds since
\mathfrak{q}=\mathbf{q}(\xi)
, while
\ast
holds because both skew-derivations act in the same way on the generators of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
.
The inclusion holds since
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\subset\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}})
: indeed
\varsigma_{i}^{\mathfrak{q}}(x)=K_{i}xK_{i}^{-1}=x
for all
x\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
.
From this inclusion and (
4.28
), it follows that
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
. The proof for
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
is analogous.
The restriction of the Poisson structure to
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0\pm}
vanishes by the definition (
2.2
).

Next we prove the statement for
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
.
Let
\beta,\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
. We have

\displaystyle\{e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},K_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}\}

\displaystyle=\overline{\frac{[E_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},K_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}]}{\nu-\xi}}=\overline{\frac{1-\mathbf{q}_{\beta\gamma}^{N_{\beta}N_{\gamma}}}{\nu-\xi}E_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}K_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}}\overset{\eqref{eq:qalphabeta-factorization}}{=}\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi)e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}K_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}.

If
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
and
\eta
is as in (
6.3
),
then
K_{\eta}^{N_{\eta}}\in\mathcal{Z}(U_{\mathfrak{q}})
and
\{e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},K_{\eta}^{N_{\eta}}\}\in\mathbb{C}e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}K_{\eta}^{N_{\eta}}\subset Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
.
This proves the claim in light of Proposition
4.8
and Property (I) in §
6.1
.
Similarly, one shows that

\displaystyle\{e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}\}

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{C}e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}},

\displaystyle\{f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}\}

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{C}e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}},

\displaystyle\{f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}\}

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{C}f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}},

for all
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+},\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
This finishes the proof for
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and reduces that of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}

to proving that
\{e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},f_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}\}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
for all
\beta,\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
.
For this we use the enumeration of the positive roots using the longest element of the Weyl groupoid.
First we assume that
\beta=\beta_{j}
,
\gamma=\beta_{k}
for
1\leq j<k\leq\ell
. Let
\mathfrak{p}=\rho_{i_{j}}\dots\rho_{i_{1}}(\mathfrak{q})
,
\gamma^{\prime}=s_{i_{j}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\dots s_{i_{1}}(\gamma)
,
so
N_{\gamma^{\prime}}=N_{\gamma}
. We have that

\displaystyle\{e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},f_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}\}

\displaystyle=\overline{\frac{[E_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},F_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}]}{\nu-\xi}}=\overline{\frac{T_{i_{1}}^{\mathbf{q}}\dots T_{i_{j}}([K_{i_{j}}^{-N_{i_{j}}}F_{i_{j}}^{N_{i_{j}}},F_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{N_{\gamma^{\prime}}}])}{\nu-\xi}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:eval-commutes-Ti}}{=}T_{i_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}\dots T_{i_{j}}\left(\overline{\frac{[K_{i_{j}}^{-N_{i_{j}}}F_{i_{j}}^{N_{i_{j}}},F_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{N_{\gamma^{\prime}}}]}{\nu-\xi}}\right)=T_{i_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}\dots T_{i_{j}}\left(\left\{K_{i_{j}}^{-N_{i_{j}}}f_{i_{j}}^{N_{i_{j}}},f_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{N_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\right\}\right).

By the statements already proved,
\left\{K_{i_{j}}^{-N_{i_{j}}}f_{i_{j}}^{N_{i_{j}}},f_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{N_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\right\}\in Z_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Hence

\displaystyle\{e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},f_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}\}

\displaystyle\in T_{i_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}\dots T_{i_{j}}(Z_{\mathfrak{p}})\overset{\text{Theorem \ref{th:Zq-invariant-under-Ti}}}{=}Z_{\mathfrak{q}}.

The case
j>k
is proved analogously. Now assume that
\beta=\gamma
. We start with the case
\beta=\alpha_{i}
for some
i\in\mathbb{I}
(a simple Cartan root). Using (
4.6
) we prove recursively that

(6.10)

\displaystyle[E_{i}^{N},F_{i}^{N}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{N}(t)_{\mathbf{q}_{ii}}^{!}\binom{N}{t}^{2}_{\mathbf{q}_{ii}}F_{i}^{N-t}\prod_{s=0}^{t-1}\left(K_{i}\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{2t-2N-s}-L_{i}^{-1}\right)E_{i}^{N-t},

\displaystyle N\in\mathbb{N}.

Let
t\in\mathbb{I}_{N_{i}-1}
. As
q_{ii}
is a primitive
N_{i}
-th root of unity and
\overline{\mathbf{q}_{ii}}=q_{ii}
,

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\overline{\frac{1-\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{N_{i}^{2}}}{\nu-\xi}}=\overline{\frac{(1-\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{N_{i}})(1+\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{N_{i}}+\cdots+\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)})}{\nu-\xi}}=N_{i}\overline{\frac{1-\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{N_{i}}}{\nu-\xi}}.

Hence,

(6.11)

\displaystyle\overline{\frac{(N_{i})_{\mathbf{q}_{ii}}^{!}}{\nu-\xi}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\frac{1-\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{N_{i}}}{\nu-\xi}}\cdot\frac{(1-q_{ii})\ldots(1-q_{ii}^{N_{i}-1})}{(1-q_{ii})^{N_{i}}}=\frac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}}(\xi)}{(1-q_{ii})^{N_{i}}}\cdot

From this we obtain,

\displaystyle\{e_{i}^{N_{i}},f_{i}^{N_{i}}\}

\displaystyle=\overline{\frac{[E_{i}^{N_{i}},F_{i}^{N_{i}}]}{\nu-\xi}}=\overline{\frac{(N_{i})_{\mathbf{q}_{ii}}^{!}}{\nu-\xi}}\prod_{s=0}^{N_{i}-1}(K_{i}q_{ii}^{-s}-L_{i})=\frac{-\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}}(\xi)}{(q_{ii}-1)^{N_{i}}}(K_{i}^{N_{i}}-L_{i}^{-N_{i}})\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}.

Next, if
\beta
is not simple, say
\beta=\beta_{j}
for some
j\in\mathbb{I}_{\ell}
, then using
Theorem
4.7
and (
5.5
) again

(6.12)

\displaystyle\{e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}\}

\displaystyle=T_{i_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}\dots T_{i_{j-1}}\Big(\overline{\frac{[E_{i_{j}}^{N_{i_{j}}},F_{i_{j}}^{N_{i_{j}}}]}{\nu-\xi}}\Big)=\frac{-\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)}{(q_{\beta\beta}-1)^{N_{\beta}}}(K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}-L_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}.\qed

7.
The associated Poisson algebraic groups

In this section we describe the Poisson algebraic groups that correspond to the Poisson-Hopf algebras

Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
.
We prove that, as algebraic groups, they are isomorphic to Borel subgroups of connected semisimple algebraic groups but of adjoint type
(and not of simply connected type as in previous works) and direct products of such Borel subgroups.
The dual Lie bialgebras of the three tangent Lie algebras are proved to constitute a Manin triple,
the ample Lie algebra in which is reductive. It is shown that the resulting Lie bialgebra structures are
the ones from the Belavin–Drinfeld classification
[
11
]
for the empty BD-triple
and arbitrary choice of the continuous parameters.
The results completely determine the
Poisson structures on the three kinds of algebraic groups in question.

7.1.
The positive and negative parts of the dual tangent Lie bialgebra of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}

Let
M_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm 0}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
be the complex
algebraic groups which are
equal to the maximal spectra of the commutative Hopf algebras

Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm 0}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
, respectively.
Here the Hopf algebra structures on
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
are the restrictions of the braided Hopf algebra structures
on
U^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{q}}
to
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}

[
9
]
.

Since
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a finitely generated Poisson-Hopf algebra which is an integral domain,

M_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a connected Poisson algebraic group (see §
B.2
for background).
Analogously,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm 0}
are connected Poisson algebraic groups,
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
are connected unipotent algebraic groups. The latter are not Poisson algebraic groups;
they are isomorphic to certain Poisson homogeneous spaces for
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
(see §
8.3
).
The tensor product decompositions

Z_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\otimes Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

from §
4.5
give rise to the decomposition of algebraic groups

(7.1)

M_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}.

This is not a direct product decomposition of Poisson algebraic groups (because
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\otimes Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

is a tensor product decomposition of commutative but not Poisson algebras). However,
the canonical projections
M_{\mathfrak{q}}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

are homomorphisms of Poisson algebraic groups because
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
are
Poisson-Hopf subalgebras of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Denote by
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
the tangent Lie bialgebras of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

(see the Appendix
B
for background and notations). Eq. (
7.1
) gives rise to the direct sum decomposition of Lie algebras

\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\oplus\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}.

The Lie coalgebra structure on
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, fully described below, has cross terms.
The dual of the tangent Lie bialgebra
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}=T^{*}_{1}M_{\mathfrak{q}}
is computed as the linearization at the identity element
1
of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}

of its Poisson structure by using (
B.1
).
The maximal ideal
\mathfrak{M}_{1}
of
\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}]\simeq Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
of functions vanishing at 1 coincides with the
augmentation ideal of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
. In the proofs below we will use the identification
T^{*}_{1}M\simeq\mathfrak{M}_{1}/\mathfrak{M}_{1}^{2}

where the differential
d_{1}(g)
of a function
g\in\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}]
at
1\in M_{\mathfrak{q}}

is sent to the class of
g-g(1)
in
\mathfrak{M}_{1}/\mathfrak{M}_{1}^{2}
for
g\in\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}]
.
The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
has the
\mathbb{C}
-basis:

(7.2)

\displaystyle\big\{d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}):\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+},\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big\}.

By Proposition
6.3
, the subspaces

(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+})^{*}:=\oplus_{\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}}\mathbb{C}\,d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-})^{*}:=\oplus_{\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}}\mathbb{C}\,d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})

are Lie subalgebras of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
. The dual Lie bialgebras
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}

are canonically identified with the Lie sub-bialgebras of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}

(7.3)

(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+})^{*}\oplus\big(\oplus_{\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})\big)\quad\mbox{and}\quad(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-})^{*}\oplus\big(\oplus_{\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})\big).

Recall the notation from §
5.4
.
It follows from the triangular decomposition (
3.15
) of the semisimple Lie algebra

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
associated to
\mathfrak{q}
that
the set of simple roots of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
can be identified with
\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Denote the entries of the Cartan matrix of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
by

\displaystyle c_{\underline{\beta}\,\underline{\gamma}},

\displaystyle\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}.

Throughout the section we will assume the identification
\mathfrak{n}_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{(-1)}}^{-}\simeq\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
from (
5.7
), so

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
will be identified with
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}^{(-1)}}^{-}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
.
By the definitions of
\mathfrak{n}_{{\mathfrak{q}}^{(-1)}}^{-}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
has a set of Chevalley generators

\displaystyle\{x_{\beta},y_{\beta},h_{\beta}:\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}\}

such that
x_{\beta}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}\iota^{*}(\theta_{\beta})
and
y_{\beta}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}\iota^{*}(\eta_{\beta})
, respectively
(here
\eta_{\beta}
and
\theta_{\beta}
are defined in (
5.6
) and the subsequent paragraph).
In this way the
root lattice of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
is identified with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
by setting
\deg x_{\beta}=-\deg y_{\beta}=N_{\beta}\beta
,

\deg h_{\beta}=0
for
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
.

We will need the following reductive Lie algebra

\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}:=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\mathbb{C},&\mbox{if $\mathfrak{q}$ is of type ${\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)$}\\
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

See the comments after Theorem
6.2
.
By (
3.15
), it has the triangular decomposition

\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}

where the Cartan subalgebra is given by

\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}:=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\mathbb{C},&\mbox{if $\mathfrak{q}$ is of type ${\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)$}\\
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In the
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
case denote by
h_{\eta}
a non-zero central element of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
In that case we have
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\mathbb{C}h_{\eta}
and

\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\mathbb{C}h_{\eta}
.

Proposition 7.1
.

We have a
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
-graded Lie algebra isomorphism

(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm})^{*}\simeq\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
given by

\displaystyle d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})\mapsto s_{\beta}\iota^{*}(\theta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})}),

respectively

\displaystyle d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})\mapsto-s_{\beta}\iota^{*}(\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})})

for all
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
, where
s_{\beta}:=\dfrac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)}{(1-q_{\beta\beta})^{N_{\beta}}}
.
In the plus case we use the identification (
5.7
).

Proof.

First we prove the minus case. Let
\beta,\gamma\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
. Since
\overline{F_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}}=f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},\overline{F_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}}=f_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}\in Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
and the subalgebra
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
is closed under the Poisson bracket
\{\cdot,\cdot\}
by Proposition
6.3
, using Proposition
5.4
we obtain

(7.4)

\displaystyle[F_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},F_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}]\equiv\sum_{\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}}(\nu-\xi)a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\nu)F_{\delta}^{N_{\delta}}+(\nu-\xi)g_{\beta\gamma}\mod(\nu-\xi)^{2}U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{-},

where
a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\nu)\in\mathsf{A}
and
g_{\beta\gamma}
is a non-commutative polynomial in
\{F_{\delta}^{N_{\delta}}:\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}
involving monomials of degree
\geq 2
. Since
U_{\mathbf{q}}
is
\mathbb{Z}^{\mathbb{I}}
-graded, the sum in the right-hand side has at most one non-zero term, when
N_{\beta}\beta+N_{\gamma}\gamma=N_{\delta}\delta
for some
\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
. Therefore

(7.5)

\displaystyle[d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),d_{1}(f_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}})]

\displaystyle=d_{1}\big(\{f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},f_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}\}\big)=d_{1}\Bigg(\,\overline{\frac{[F_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}},F_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}]}{\nu-\xi}}\,\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}}a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\xi)d_{1}(f_{\delta}^{N_{\delta}})+d_{1}(g_{\beta\gamma})=\sum_{\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}}a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\xi)d_{1}(f_{\delta}^{N_{\delta}}),

because
g_{\beta\gamma}\in{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}^{2}
. From (
7.4
) and since
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{-}
is
\mathbb{N}_{0}
-graded connected, we see that

\displaystyle[F_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})},F_{\gamma}^{(N_{\gamma})}]\equiv\sum_{\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}}a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\nu)\frac{(\nu-\xi)(N_{\delta})_{q_{\delta\delta}}^{!}}{(N_{\beta})_{q_{\beta\beta}}^{!}(N_{\gamma})_{q_{\gamma\gamma}}^{!}}F_{\delta}^{(N_{\delta})}\mod(\nu-\xi)U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}.

It follows from (
6.11
) that

\displaystyle\overline{\frac{(N_{\beta})_{q_{\beta\beta}}^{!}}{\nu-\xi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)}{(1-q_{\beta\beta})^{N_{\beta}}}=s_{\beta}.

Hence in
U_{\mathbf{q},\mathsf{A}}^{\operatorname{res}-}/(\nu-\xi)
we have

(7.6)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &=\sum_{\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}}a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\xi)s_{\delta}\overline{F_{\delta}^{(N_{\delta})}}\\
&=\begin{cases}a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\xi)s_{\delta}\overline{F_{\delta}^{(N_{\delta})}}&\text{ if }\exists\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}:\underline{\delta}=\underline{\beta}+\underline{\gamma},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{aligned}

The statement of the lemma follows from this identity, (
7.5
) and Proposition
5.9
.
The plus case is proved analogously, using Remark
5.8
and that
\mathfrak{q}_{\beta\gamma}=1
for all
\beta,\gamma\in\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
.
∎

The last part of the proof gives the following fact about the structure of Lusztig algebras which is of independent interest. Recall

\eta_{\beta}
defined in (
5.6
).

Corollary 7.2
.

The braided Hopf algebra projection
\iota^{*}:\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}\twoheadrightarrow U(\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-})
(recall (
3.18
))
has an algebra section
U(\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-})\to\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}
given by

\displaystyle\iota^{*}(\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})})

\displaystyle\mapsto\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})},

\displaystyle\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.9
and (
7.6
),

(7.7)

\displaystyle[\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})},\eta_{\gamma}^{(N_{\gamma})}]

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\tfrac{a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\xi)s_{\delta}}{s_{\beta}s_{\gamma}}\eta_{\delta}^{(N_{\delta})},&\text{ if }\exists\delta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}:\underline{\delta}=\underline{\beta}+\underline{\gamma},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

On the other hand, set
\mathtt{x}_{\underline{\beta}}:=\iota^{*}(\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})})
. As
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
is the positive part of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and each
\mathtt{x}_{\underline{\beta}}
has weight
\underline{\beta}
, there exist
a_{\underline{\beta}\,\underline{\gamma}}\in\Bbbk
,
\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma}\in\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
, such that

(7.8)

\displaystyle[\mathtt{x}_{\underline{\beta}},\mathtt{x}_{\underline{\gamma}}]

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a_{\underline{\beta}\,\underline{\gamma}}\mathtt{x}_{\underline{\beta}+\underline{\gamma}},&\underline{\beta}+\underline{\gamma}\in\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+},\\
0,&\underline{\beta}+\underline{\gamma}\notin\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}.\end{cases}

Applying
\iota^{*}
to (
7.7
) we obtain that
a_{\underline{\beta}\,\underline{\gamma}}=\tfrac{a_{\beta\gamma}^{\delta}(\xi)s_{\delta}}{s_{\beta}s_{\gamma}}

for each pair
\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma}\in\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
such that
\underline{\beta}+\underline{\gamma}\in\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
.
Therefore the existence of the algebra map
U(\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-})\to\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}
as above follows since
U(\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-})
is presented by generators
\mathtt{x}_{\underline{\beta}}
,
\underline{\beta}\in\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
, and relations (
7.8
), and the corresponding relations for
\eta_{\beta}^{(N_{\beta})}
hold in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{q}}

by (
7.7
).
∎

7.2.
The dual tangent Lie bialgebra of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}

Lemma 7.3
.

The following equalities hold in the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
:

\displaystyle[d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),d_{1}(f_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}})]

\displaystyle=-\delta_{\beta\gamma}\frac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)}{(q_{\beta\beta}-1)^{N_{\beta}}}(d_{1}(K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})+d_{1}(L_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})),

\displaystyle\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}},

and

\displaystyle[d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})]

\displaystyle=-\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\beta}(\xi)d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),

\displaystyle[d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})]

\displaystyle=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\beta}(\xi)d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),

\displaystyle[d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})]

\displaystyle=-\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\mu}(\xi)d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),

\displaystyle[d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})]

\displaystyle=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\mu}(\xi)d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})

for all
\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}
,
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}

Proof.

The case of
\underline{\beta}\neq\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
of the first identity follows from the fact that (
7.2
) is a basis of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}

and that the latter is
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
-graded.
The case
\underline{\beta}=\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
is a consequence of (
6.12
) since

d_{1}(L_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})=-d_{1}(L_{\beta}^{-N_{\beta}})
, which in turn follows since the value of
L_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}

at the identity of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}
equals
1
.
The other four identities follow from (
4.4
)–(
4.5
).
∎

Since the polynomials
\nu^{n}-a
are separable over
\mathbb{C}
for
a\neq 0
, we infer from (
6.1
) that

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)\neq 0

\displaystyle\mbox{for all}\;\;\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}.

Theorem 7.4
.

(a)

The Cartan matrix of the semisimple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
is given by

\displaystyle c_{\underline{\beta}\,\underline{\gamma}}=\frac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi)+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\beta}(\xi)}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)},

\displaystyle\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}.

(b)

There is a (
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
-graded) Lie algebra isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
such that

\displaystyle x_{\beta}\mapsto d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),\;\;y_{\beta}\mapsto\frac{(q_{\beta}-1)^{N_{\beta}}}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)^{2}}d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),\;\;h_{\mu}\mapsto\frac{1}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\mu}(\xi)}(d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})+d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}))

for
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
,
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
,
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
maps to the subspace

\big\{\sum_{{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}a_{\mu}d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})+b_{\mu}d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}):\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi)a_{\mu}+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\mu}(\xi)b_{\mu}=0,\forall\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big\}

of the abelian Lie algebra
\oplus_{\underline{\beta}\in\widetilde{\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\mathbb{C}d_{1}(K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})+\mathbb{C}d_{1}(L_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}))
.

Proof.

(a) For
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
and
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
,
define the following elements of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
:

\displaystyle\widehat{x}_{\beta}:=d_{1}(f_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}),\;\;\widehat{y}_{\beta}:=\frac{(q_{\beta}-1)^{N_{\beta}}}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)^{2}}d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),\;\;\widehat{h}_{\mu}:=\frac{1}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\mu}(\xi)}(d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})+d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}))

and the Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta):=\mathbb{C}\widehat{x}_{\beta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\widehat{h}_{\beta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\widehat{y}_{\beta}
. Lemma
7.3

implies that, for all
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
,
[\widehat{h}_{\beta},\widehat{x}_{\beta}]=2\widehat{x}_{\beta}
,
[\widehat{h}_{\beta},\widehat{y}_{\beta}]=-2\widehat{y}_{\beta}
,

[\widehat{x}_{\beta},\widehat{y}_{\beta}]=\widehat{h}_{\beta}
, so

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta)\simeq{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
.

Now take
\underline{\beta}\neq\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
and consider
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
as a
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta)
-module under the adjoint action.
It follows from Lemma
7.3
that

\displaystyle[\widehat{x}_{\beta},\widehat{y}_{\gamma}]=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad[\widehat{h}_{\beta},\widehat{y}_{\gamma}]=-\frac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi)+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\beta}(\xi)}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)}\widehat{x}_{\gamma},

so
\widehat{y}_{\gamma}
is a highest weight vector for
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta)\simeq{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
of weight

-\frac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi)+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\beta}(\xi)}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)}\omega
where

\omega
denotes the fundamental weight of
{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
. The isomorphism of Proposition
7.1
and the Serre relations in

\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
imply that

\displaystyle\operatorname{ad}^{-c_{\beta\gamma}+1}_{\widehat{y}_{\beta}}(\widehat{y}_{\gamma})=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\operatorname{ad}^{j}_{\widehat{y}_{\beta}}(\widehat{y}_{\gamma})\neq 0\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;j\leqslant-c_{\beta\gamma}.

Hence,
\operatorname{ad}^{-c_{\beta\gamma}}_{\widehat{y}_{\beta}}(\widehat{y}_{\gamma})
is the lowest weight vector of the (irreducible)

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\beta)
-module generated by
\widehat{y}_{\gamma}
, which forces

c_{\underline{\beta}\,\underline{\gamma}}=\frac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi)+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\beta}(\xi)}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)}\cdot

This proves part (a). It also proves that the assignment
x_{\beta}\mapsto\widehat{x}_{\beta}
,

y_{\beta}\mapsto\widehat{y}_{\beta}
,
h_{\beta}\mapsto\widehat{h}_{\beta}
for
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
defines
a
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
-graded Lie algebra homomorphism
\varphi:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}\to\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
which is an
embedding by Proposition
7.1
and the linear independence of

\{d_{1}(K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),d_{1}(L_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}):\beta\in\mathfrak{O}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}\}
.
Here we use the canonical isomorphism
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}\to\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\mp}

obtained by restricting the Chevalley involution of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
.

If
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
, then
\widehat{h}_{\eta}
is in the center of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
by
(
6.5
) and Lemma
7.3
. Furthermore,
\widehat{h}_{\eta}\notin\varphi(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}})

by the definition of
d(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})
and
d(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})
for
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Hence,
\varphi
extends to an embedding

(7.9)

\varphi:\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\to\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}

by setting
\varphi(h_{\eta}):=\widehat{h}_{\eta}
if
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
. Denote

(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{*}:=\oplus_{\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}\Big(\mathbb{C}d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})\oplus\mathbb{C}d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})\Big).

Let
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{\prime}
be the intersection of the kernels of the functionals

\{l_{\gamma}:\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}\}
on
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{*}
given by

\displaystyle l_{\gamma}(d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})):=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi),

\displaystyle l_{\gamma}(d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})):=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\mu}(\xi).

Part (a) of the theorem, the constructed embedding (
7.9
), and eq. (
6.6
) imply that

(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{\prime}\cap\operatorname{Im}\varphi=0
. Hence,
\dim(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{\prime}\leq\dim(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{*}-\dim\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\dim\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Since the number of the above functionals equals
|\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}|=\dim\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, we have

\dim(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{\prime}\geqslant\dim\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}.

It follows from part (a) that
\dim(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{\prime}\leqslant\dim\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, Hence

\dim\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}\geq\dim(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{*}-\dim\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\dim\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}.

Therefore
\dim(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{\prime}=\dim\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}}=\dim(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{*}/2
and
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}=\varphi(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\eta})\oplus(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{\prime}
.
Taking a vector space isomorphism
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0})^{\prime}
and combining it with the embedding
\varphi
, gives the needed Lie algebra isomorphism for part (b).
∎

Let
(\cdot,\cdot)
be the invariant symmetric bilinear form on
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
for which the induced form on the dual of the Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}

satisfies
(\underline{\beta},\underline{\beta})=2
for short roots
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
We extend it to a non-degenerate invariant symmetric bilinear form on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
where in the case when
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
we let

\displaystyle(h_{\eta},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}})=0,

\displaystyle(h_{\eta},h_{\eta})=2.

In this case we identify

\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\cong\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}\oplus\mathbb{C}\underline{\eta},

where
\underline{\eta}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
is such that
\langle\underline{\eta},h_{\eta}\rangle=2
and
\langle\underline{\eta},h_{\underline{\beta}}\rangle=0

for all
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
.

We will identify
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
with
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}

via the bilinear form
(.,.)
. Define the scalars

\kappa_{\mu}:=2\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\mu}(\xi)(\underline{\mu},\underline{\mu})^{-1}\quad\quad\mbox{for}\quad\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}.

For
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
, the scalar
\kappa_{\beta}

only depends on the simple factor of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
of which
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
is a root,
because by Theorem
7.4
(a),

(7.10)

c_{\beta\gamma}=\frac{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi)+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\beta}(\xi)}{\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\beta}(\xi)}=\frac{2(\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma})}{(\underline{\beta},\underline{\beta})}\cdot

If
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
, then
(\underline{\eta},\underline{\eta})=2
.

Proposition
A.3
(i) tells us that
each large quantum group
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
is realized as a specialization of an integral form of a one-parameter quantum
group
U_{\mathbf{q}}
in infinitely many different ways parametrized by integers
t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
for
i<j\in\mathbb{I}
.
Furthermore, by part (ii) of that proposition, for a generic choice of the parameters
t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
i<j\in\mathbb{I}
,
the matrix with entries
\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\beta\gamma}(\xi)
for
\underline{\beta},\underline{\gamma}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
is non-degenerate.
In the remaining part of the paper we will assume the following:

Non-degeneracy Assumption 7.5
.

The specialization parameters
t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
i<j\in\mathbb{I}
in
Proposition
A.3
are chosen in such a way that
the matrix
{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}
in (
6.2
) is non-degenerate.

It follows from (
6.5
)–(
6.6
) that the matrix

\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\mathbf{q}}:=(\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi))_{\underline{\mu},\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}

is invertible.

Remark 7.6
.

In what follows we will identify the Lie algebras

(7.11)

\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}

via
the isomorphism from Theorem
7.4
. In particular,
x_{\beta},y_{\beta},h_{\mu}
for
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}},\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}

will be viewed as elements of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
.
We also fix the identification of abelian Lie algebras

(7.12)

\big\{\sum_{\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}a_{\mu}d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})+b_{\mu}d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}):\sum_{\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi)a_{\mu}+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\mu}(\xi)b_{\mu}=0,\forall\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}\big\}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}

for Theorem
7.4
(b)
by sending

\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}a_{\mu}d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})+b_{\mu}d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})\mapsto\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}b_{\mu}\kappa_{\mu}\underline{\mu}
,
using the identification of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
with
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
via the form
(.,.)
.
Since both Lie algebras in (
7.12
) have the same dimensions, we only need to show that this map is injective. An element in its kernel
has
b_{\mu}=0
for all
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
and thus,

\sum\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi)a_{\mu}=0
for all
\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
The Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
implies that
a_{\mu}=0
for
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Consider the Borel subalgebras
\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}:=\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
. We have

(7.13)

(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}

in the identification (
7.3
) of
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}

with Lie subalgebras of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
.
Using the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
one more time, we obtain
that the projection into the first component
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
restricts to the Lie algebra isomorphisms

(7.14)

(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\quad\mbox{and}\quad(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}.

We next describe the embeddings (
7.13
). Denote the linear maps
\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}},\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\,\mathrm{T}}\in\operatorname{End}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}})
:

(7.15)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}(\underline{\mu}):=\sum_{{\underline{\gamma}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi)\underline{\gamma},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\,\mathrm{T}}(\underline{\mu}):=\sum_{{\underline{\gamma}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\mu}(\xi)\underline{\gamma}.

Because of the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
, the matrix
\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\mathbf{q}}
is invertible,
and thus both endomorphisms are invertible.

Denote by
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
the invariant symmetric bilinear form on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, which is a rescaling
of
(\cdot,\cdot)
by
\kappa_{\mu}^{-1}
on each simple factor of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and on the one-dimensional
center of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
if
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
. It satisfies

\displaystyle(\!(d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})+d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(K_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}})+d_{1}(L_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}))\!)=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\mu}(\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\gamma}(\xi)(\!(h_{\mu},h_{\gamma})\!)

\displaystyle=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\mu}(\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\gamma}(\xi)\kappa_{\gamma}^{-1}\frac{2c_{\mu\gamma}}{(\underline{\gamma},\underline{\gamma})}=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi)+\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\mu}(\xi),\quad\forall\underline{\mu},\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}.\cdot

This implies that the form
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
has a unique extension to an invariant symmetric bilinear form on
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
such that

(7.16)

\displaystyle(\!(d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(L_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}))\!)=\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi),

(7.17)

\displaystyle(\!(d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}))\!)=(\!(d_{1}(K_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}),d_{1}(L_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}))\!)=0

for
\underline{\mu},\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
. The Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
implies that the bilinear
form
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
on
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
is non-degenerate.

One easily verifies that the orthogonal complement in
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
equals
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Proposition 7.7
.

For all large quantum groups
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
satisfying the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
,
the subalgebras
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}

and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
are given by

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}=\{(y+h,-h):y\in\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-},h\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\},

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}=\{(x+h,{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}{\mathscr{P}}^{\,\mathrm{T}}(h)):x\in\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+},h\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\}.

Proof.

Denote the first (abelian) Lie algebra in (
7.12
) by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{(2)}
. Fix

\displaystyle h:=\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}c_{\mu}(d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})+d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})),

\displaystyle h_{1}:=\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}a_{\mu}d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),

\displaystyle h_{2}:=\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}b_{\mu}d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}).

If
h_{1}+h_{2}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{(2)}
, then
l_{\gamma}(h_{1})=-l_{\gamma}(h_{2})
for all
\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
, which is equivalent to

(7.18)

\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}\big(\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}a_{\mu}\underline{\mu}\big)=-\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\,\mathrm{T}}\big(\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}b_{\mu}\underline{\mu}\big).

By Theorem
7.4
(b), in the identification (
7.11
),
d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})+d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})

corresponds to
\kappa_{\mu}\underline{\mu}
for all
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Hence, the first statement of the proposition is equivalent to proving that for all

h
,
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
as above, if
h_{1}+h_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{(2)}
and
h+h_{1}+h_{2}\in(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
,
then
c_{\mu}=-b_{\mu}
for
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
From the condition
h_{1}+h_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{(2)}
we obtain
(\!(d_{1}(L_{\gamma}^{N_{\gamma}}),h+h_{2})\!)=0
for all
\underline{\gamma}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}}\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\mu\gamma}(\xi)(c_{\mu}+b_{\mu})=0,

\displaystyle\forall{\underline{\gamma}}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}.

Now the first statement of the proposition follows from the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
.
The second one follows from the first by interchanging the roles of
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}
