and applying
(
7.18
).
∎

We next describe the Lie coalgebra structure on
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
and the
corresponding Manin triple; see §
B
for background.

Theorem 7.8
.

For every choice of the specialization parameters

t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
the following hold:

(a)

The Lie coalgebra structure of the Lie bialgebra
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
is given by

\displaystyle\delta(x_{\beta})=d_{1}(L_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})\wedge x_{\beta},

\displaystyle\delta(y_{\beta})=d_{1}(K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})\wedge y_{\beta},

\displaystyle\delta(d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}))=\delta(d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}))=0

for all
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}},\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.

(b)

With respect to the bilinear form

(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
,
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*},(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*},(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*})
is a Manin triple.

(c)

The Lie coalgebra structures of
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}
satisfy

(7.19)

\displaystyle(\!(\delta(y),x_{1}\otimes x_{2})\!)=-(\!(y,[x_{1},x_{2}])\!),

\displaystyle(\!(\delta(x),y_{1}\otimes y_{2})\!)=(\!(x,[y_{1},y_{2}])\!)

for all
x,x_{1},x_{2}\in(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}
and
y,y_{1},y_{2}\in(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
.

Remark 7.9
.

(a)

Part (a) of the theorem uniquely determines the Lie coalgebra structures of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
,
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
,
since the set

\{x_{\beta},y_{\beta},d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}),d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}):\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}},\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}\}

and its appropriate subsets generate the Lie algebras
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
,
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
.

(b)

By part (c) of the theorem, the Lie coalgebra structures of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
,
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
,
are precisely the ones that are associated to a Manin triple as in Remark
B.1
(c). In particular,
we have the isomorphism of Lie bialgebras

(7.20)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}\simeq D((\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}),

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}\simeq(((\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*})^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{\mathrm{op}}.

(c)

The Lie bialgebra structures on the reductive Lie algebras
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
from part (a) of
the theorem correspond to empty Belavin–Drinfeld triples
and arbitrary
choice of the continuous parameters in their classification
[
11
]
.

Proof of Theorem
7.8
.

Part (a) follows from Lemma
B.2
and the identities

(7.21)

\displaystyle\Delta(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})

\displaystyle=K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}\otimes e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}+e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}\otimes 1,

\displaystyle\Delta(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})

\displaystyle=1\otimes f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}+f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}\otimes L_{\beta}^{-N_{\beta}}.

for
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
and the fact that
K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}
and
L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}}
for
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}

are group-like elements.

(b) The subalgebras
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}
are orthogonal to their nilradicals because of the embeddings
(
7.13
).
This, combined with (
7.17
), implies that they are isotropic subalgebras of
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}
with respect to the form

(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
. The direct sum decomposition
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\oplus\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
yields the desired result.

(c) Part (a) of the theorem and the isomorphism in Theorem
7.4
(b) imply at once the validity of the identities
(
7.19
) for
y=d_{1}(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})
,
y=d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})
,

x=d_{1}(f_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})
,
x=d_{1}(L_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})
, where
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}

\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
,
and for all possible choices of
x_{1},x_{2},y_{2},y_{2}
.
The general case follows by induction on root height when
x
,
y
are chosen to be root vectors
by using the invariance of the bilinear form
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
.
∎

7.3.
The Poisson algebraic groups
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

Combining the isomorphisms (
7.14
) and (
7.20
), we get the Lie algebra isomorphisms

(7.22)

\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\simeq((\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*})_{\mathrm{op}}\simeq(\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+})_{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\simeq(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-},

where
(.)_{\mathrm{op}}
stands for the opposite Lie algebra structure and
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+})_{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}

is the standard Lie algebra isomorphism
x\mapsto-x
.
The proof of Proposition
7.7
shows that the corresponding pull back maps on the level of duals send

(7.23)

\displaystyle\underline{\beta}\mapsto-d_{1}(K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),

\displaystyle\underline{\beta}\mapsto d_{1}(L_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}),

\displaystyle\forall\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}.

The scalars
\kappa_{\beta}
do not appear here because the form
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
is a rescaling of the form
(\cdot,\cdot)

by
\kappa_{\mu}^{-1}
on each simple factor of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and on the one-dimensional
center of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
if
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
.

Denote by
G_{\mathfrak{q}}
the adjoint semisimple algebraic group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
. Let

\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\begin{cases}G_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times},&\mbox{if $\mathfrak{q}$ is of type ${\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)$}\\
G_{\mathfrak{q}},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In the former case the exponential map

(7.24)

\exp:\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\to\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\quad\mbox{is given by}\quad\exp(x+ch_{\eta})=(\exp(x),\exp(c)),

where in the first component in the right hand side we use the exponential
map
\exp:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}\to G_{\mathfrak{q}}
, and
x\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
c\in\mathbb{C}
.
Denote by
\widetilde{B}^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{q}}
the Borel subgroups of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
corresponding to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}

and by
B^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{q}}
the Borel subgroups of
G_{\mathfrak{q}}
corresponding to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{b}}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
We have
\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}=B_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
if
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
,
and
\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}=B_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
otherwise.

Let
\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}:=\widetilde{B}^{+}_{\mathfrak{q}}\cap\widetilde{B}^{-}_{\mathfrak{q}}
be the corresponding maximal torus of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Denote by
N_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}\subset G_{\mathfrak{q}}
the unipotent radicals of
\widetilde{B}^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

The groups of group-like elements of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
are the free abelian groups
on
K_{\mu}^{\pm N_{\mu}}
,
\underline{\beta}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
and
L_{\mu}^{\pm N_{\mu}}
,
\mu\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
,
respectively.

Theorem 7.10
.

For every choice of the specialization parameters

t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
,
the Lie algebra isomorphisms (
7.22
) integrate to isomorphisms of algebraic groups.

\tau_{+}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tau_{-}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}.

Theorem
7.10
describes explicitly the algebraic groups
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
. As an algebraic group,

M_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\times\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
. The Poisson structures on
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}

are the unique Poisson algebraic group structures that integrate the Lie bialgebras
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, whose
dual Lie bialgebras are described in Theorem
7.8
.

Proof.

We prove the first statement, the second being analogous.
Since
G_{\mathfrak{q}}
is of adjoint type, the Borel subgroup
B_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
is canonically identified with the identity component of
\operatorname{Aut}(\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+})
. The adjoint action of

M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
on
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\simeq\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
induces a surjective homomorphism

\tau_{+}^{1}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\twoheadrightarrow B_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
. If
\mathfrak{q}
is of type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)
, then we also have a canonical
surjective homomorphism
\tau_{+}^{2}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, whose pull back
map
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{C}^{\times}]\cong\mathbb{C}[\chi^{\pm 1}]\to\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}]

is given by
\chi\mapsto K_{\eta}^{N_{\eta}}
, where
\chi
is the identity character of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.
Define the homomorphism
\tau_{+}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
given by

\tau_{+}:=\begin{cases}(\tau_{+}^{1},\tau_{+}^{2}),&\mbox{if $\mathfrak{q}$ is of type ${\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)$}\\
\tau_{+}^{1},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

It follows from (
6.8
) that the homomorphism
\tau_{+}
is surjective. It restricts to an isomorphism

\tau_{+}:N(M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}N_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
, where
N(M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})
is the unipotent radical of

M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
. The homomorphism
\tau_{+}
also restricts to a surjective homomorphism

(7.25)

\tau_{+}:T(M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}},

where
T(M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})
is a maximal torus of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
. The tori
T(M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})
and
\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
are connected because

M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
are connected algebraic groups. In view of the Levi decompositions of

M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
, in order to prove that
\tau_{+}
is an isomorphism, it is sufficient to show that the restriction
(
7.25
) is an isomorphism. However,

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[T(M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})]\simeq\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}/N(M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})]\simeq\mathbb{C}[G(\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}])],

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}]\simeq\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}/N_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}]\simeq\mathbb{C}[G(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}])],

where
G(H)
denotes the group of group-like elements of a Hopf algebra
H
.

The group of group-like elements of

\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}]\simeq Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
is the free abelian group with generators
K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}
,
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
The group of group-like elements of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}]
equals the character lattice of
\widetilde{B}^{+}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, which is
canonically identified with the lattice
\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
The differentials at the identity element of the two generating sets are respectively
d_{1}(K_{\mu}^{N_{\mu}})
and
\underline{\mu}
,
where
\underline{\mu}\in\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}
. Eq. (
7.23
) implies that
\tau_{+}^{*}:G(\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}])\to G(\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}])
is an isomorphism.
Hence,
\tau_{+}^{*}:\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}]\to\mathbb{C}[T(M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}))]
is an isomorphism and the same holds for (
7.25
). This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Example 7.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}
be of type
\mathtt{wk}(4)
and fix
N=\operatorname{ord}q
,
M=\operatorname{ord}(-q)
, see §
A.3
.
Let
\gamma=\alpha_{1}+2\alpha_{2}+3\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}
. Then
N_{\alpha_{1}}=N_{\alpha_{2}}=N
,
N_{\alpha_{4}}=N_{\gamma}=M
,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathfrak{O}}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{+}

\displaystyle=\{\underline{\alpha}_{1},\underline{\alpha}_{2},\underline{\alpha}_{1}+\underline{\alpha}_{2},\underline{\alpha}_{4},\underline{\gamma},\underline{\alpha}_{4}+\underline{\gamma}\}.

As shown previously,
\Delta(e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}})=e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}\otimes 1+K_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}\otimes e_{\beta}^{N_{\beta}}

for
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}=\{\underline{\alpha}_{1},\underline{\alpha}_{2},\underline{\alpha}_{4},\underline{\gamma}\}
. We can check that

e_{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}=[e_{1},e_{2}]_{c}
,
e_{\alpha_{4}+\gamma}=[e_{\gamma},e_{4}]_{c}
and

\displaystyle\Delta(e_{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}^{N})=e_{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}^{N}\otimes 1+(q-1)^{N}e_{\alpha_{1}}^{N}K_{\alpha_{2}}^{N}\otimes e_{\alpha_{2}}^{N}+K_{\alpha_{1}}^{N}K_{\alpha_{2}}^{N}\otimes e_{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}^{N},

\displaystyle\Delta(e_{\alpha_{4}+\gamma}^{M})=e_{\alpha_{4}+\gamma}^{M}\otimes 1+(q+1)^{M}e_{\gamma}^{M}K_{\alpha_{4}}^{M}\otimes e_{\alpha_{4}}^{M}+K_{\alpha_{4}}^{M}K_{\gamma}^{M}\otimes e_{\alpha_{4}+\gamma}^{M}.

We now construct an explicit isomorphism between
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and the algebra of functions over the Borel subgroup of
\operatorname{PSL}_{3}(\mathbb{C})\times\operatorname{PSL}_{3}(\mathbb{C})
.
We consider the Levi decomposition
\widetilde{B}_{3}\simeq N_{3}\rtimes\widetilde{T}_{3}
of the Borel subgroup of
\operatorname{SL}_{3}(\mathbb{C})
,
where

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{3}

\displaystyle=\{\mathbf{t}=\operatorname{diag}(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}):t_{i}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times},t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}=1\},

\displaystyle N_{3}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\mathbf{n}=\begin{pmatrix}1&t_{12}&t_{13}\\
0&1&t_{12}\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}:t_{ij}\in\mathbb{C}\Big\}.

Let
a_{i},x_{ij}:\widetilde{B}_{3}\to\mathbb{C}
be the coordinate functions sending
\mathbf{t}\mapsto t_{i}

and
\mathbf{n}\mapsto t_{ij}
respectively.
The coproducts of these coordinate functions are given by
\Delta(a_{i})=a_{i}\otimes a_{i}
and

\displaystyle\Delta(x_{12})

\displaystyle=x_{12}\otimes 1+a_{1}a_{2}^{-1}\otimes x_{12},\quad\quad\Delta(x_{23})=x_{23}\otimes 1+a_{2}a_{3}^{-1}\otimes x_{23},

\displaystyle\Delta(x_{13})

\displaystyle=x_{13}\otimes 1+x_{12}a_{2}a_{3}^{-1}\otimes x_{23}+a_{1}a_{3}^{-1}\otimes x_{13}.

Denote
\mathbb{Z}_{3}=\langle(\zeta,\zeta,\zeta)\rangle
, where
\zeta
is a primitive 3rd root of unity.
The Borel subgroup
B_{3}
of

\operatorname{PSL}_{3}(\mathbb{C})
has Levi decomposition
B_{3}\simeq N_{3}\rtimes T_{3}
where
T_{3}=\widetilde{T}/\mathbb{Z}_{3}
, so

\mathbb{C}[B_{3}]=\mathbb{C}[N_{3}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{T}_{3}]^{\mathbb{Z}_{3}}=\mathbb{C}[x_{12},x_{23},x_{13},a_{12}^{\pm 1},a_{23}^{\pm 1}],

where
a_{12}:=a_{1}a_{2}^{-1}
and
a_{23}=a_{2}a_{3}^{-1}
. The coproducts of the coordinate functions on
B_{3}
are given by
\Delta(a_{ii+1})=a_{ii+1}\otimes a_{ii+1}

and

\displaystyle\Delta(x_{12})

\displaystyle=x_{12}\otimes 1+a_{12}\otimes x_{12},\quad\quad\Delta(x_{23})=x_{23}\otimes 1+a_{23}\otimes x_{23},

\displaystyle\Delta(x_{13})

\displaystyle=x_{13}\otimes 1+x_{12}a_{23}\otimes x_{23}+a_{12}a_{23}\otimes x_{13}.

The Borel subgroup of
\operatorname{PSL}_{3}(\mathbb{C})\times\operatorname{PSL}_{3}(\mathbb{C})
is isomorphic
to
B_{3}\times B_{3}
. We denote the coordinate functions
a_{ii+1}
and
x_{ij}

on the first and second copy of
B_{3}
by superscripts 1 and 2.
Now, clearly the map
\tau_{+}:Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\to\mathbb{C}[B_{3}\times B_{3}]
given by

\displaystyle K_{\alpha_{1}}^{N}

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{12}^{1},

\displaystyle K_{\alpha_{2}}^{N}

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{23}^{1},

\displaystyle K_{\alpha_{3}}^{N}

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{12}^{2},

\displaystyle K_{\gamma}^{M}

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{23}^{2}

and

\displaystyle e_{\alpha_{1}}^{N}

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{12}^{1},

\displaystyle e_{\alpha_{2}}^{N}

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{23}^{1},

\displaystyle\frac{e_{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}^{N}}{(q-1)^{N}}

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{13}^{1},

\displaystyle e_{\alpha_{4}}^{M}

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{12}^{2},

\displaystyle e_{\gamma}^{M}

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{23}^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{e_{\alpha_{4}+\gamma}^{M}}{(q+1)^{M}}

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{13}^{2}

is a Hopf algebra isomorphism.

8.
Poisson geometry and representations

In this section we describe the symplectic foliations and the torus orbits of symplectic leaves of the
Poisson algebraic groups
M_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
, and the Poisson
homogeneous spaces
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
.
Previous work in this direction dealt with the so called standard Poisson structures on simple algebraic groups (and their Borel subgroups)
[
34
]
, the dual Poisson algebraic groups
[
18
]

and the related flag varieties
[
28
]
. See also
[
15
,
19
,
35
]
.
The Poisson structures in Remark
7.9
are not of standard type in general and the results in this section
can not be deduced from
[
34
,
18
,
28
]
.
For
z\in M_{\mathfrak{q}}
, respectively
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
,
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}
, let

\mathcal{H}_{z}
, respectively
\mathcal{H}_{z}^{\geqslant}
,
\mathcal{H}_{z}^{\leqslant}
,
\mathcal{H}_{z}^{+}
,
\mathcal{H}_{z}^{-}
be the algebra defined in
Theorem
A

(c)
, respectively (
1.2
).
The Poisson geometric results described above
provide information on the irreducible representations
of the large quantum groups
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
by reduction to the sheaf of algebras
\mathcal{H}_{z}
,
z\in M_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Analogous results hold for
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
,
and
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
.

8.1.
Representations of the large quantum groups and symplectic foliations

The Manin triple described in Theorem
7.8
and the identification
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{*}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}

equip
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
with a quasitriangular Lie bialgebra structure, which turns
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
into a Poisson algebraic group.
The Poisson structure on
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}

equals
L_{g}(r)-R_{g}(r)
for
g\in\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, where
r\in\wedge^{2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}})

is the
r
-matrix for the Lie bialgebra structure on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
and
L_{g}(-)
and
R_{g}(-)
refer to the left and right-invariant bivector fields on
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Let
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
be the connected Lie subgroups of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
with Lie algebras

(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant})^{*}
and
(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*}
.
Proposition
7.7
implies that
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
is an algebraic subgroup, while

\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
is not necessarily a closed Lie subgroup.
The projection onto the first component
\pi:\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
gives the surjective Lie group homomorphisms

\displaystyle\pi_{+}:\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+},

\displaystyle\pi_{-}:\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}.

Since
G_{\mathfrak{q}}
is of adjoint type, the kernel of the exponential map
\exp:\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{q}}\twoheadrightarrow T_{\mathfrak{q}}
equals
2\pi iP_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee}
, where
P_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee}

denotes the coweight lattice of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
. It follows from (
7.24
) that the kernel of the exponential map

\exp:\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
equals
2\pi i\widetilde{P}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee}
, where
\widetilde{P}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee}

is the coweight lattice of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
given by

\widetilde{P}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee}=\begin{cases}\widetilde{P}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee}\oplus\mathbb{Z}h_{\eta},&\mbox{if $\mathfrak{q}$ is of type ${\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)$}\\
\widetilde{P}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Denote the subgroup

(8.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}:=\exp\big(2\pi i\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\,\mathrm{T}}(P_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee})\big)\subset\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}},

cf. (
7.15
).
Proposition
7.7
and the solvability of
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
give that

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}

\displaystyle=(N_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\times\{1\})\times\{\exp(h,\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\,\mathrm{T}}(h)):h\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}

\displaystyle=(N_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\times\{1\})\times\{(t,t^{-1}):t\in\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\},

from which one obtains that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ker}\pi_{+}=\{1\},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ker}\pi_{-}=\{1\}\times\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}.

Composing
\pi_{\pm}
with the isomorphisms from Theorem
7.10
leads to the isomorphisms

\displaystyle\tau_{+}^{-1}\pi_{+}:\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant},

\displaystyle\tau_{-}^{-1}\pi_{-}:\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}/\operatorname{Ker}\pi_{-}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}.

Their inverses give the canonical embeddings

(8.2)

\displaystyle j_{+}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big),

\displaystyle j_{-}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big).

Here we use that
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)\simeq\big(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)/\operatorname{Ker}\pi_{-}

and
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\cap\operatorname{Ker}\pi_{-}=\{1\}
.

Remark 8.1
.

If the matrix
\mathbf{q}
is symmetric, then so is the matrix
{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}
. This implies that

\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}=\widetilde{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\,\mathrm{T}}
and that the group
\widetilde{C}_{\mathbf{q}}
is trivial. Then
the continuous parameter accompanying the BD triple is as in Example
B.5
and the Poisson structure is the standard one.

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
be a large quantum group. For every choice of the specialization parameters

t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
the following hold:

(a)

The symplectic leaves of the
Poisson algebraic group
M_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
are the inverse images
j^{-1}(\mathcal{O}\times{t})

under the map

\displaystyle j:M_{\mathfrak{q}}\to\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big),

\displaystyle j(m_{+},m_{-}):=j_{+}(m_{+})^{-1}j_{-}(m_{-}),

\displaystyle m_{+}\in M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant},m_{-}\in M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant},

where
\mathcal{O}
is a conjugacy class of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
t\in\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}
. The dimension of the symplectic leaf
j^{-1}(\mathcal{O}\times\{t\})
equals
\dim\mathcal{O}
.

(b)

If
j(z)
and
j(z^{\prime})
are in the same conjugacy class of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)
, then there is an algebra isomorphism

\mathcal{H}_{z}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{z^{\prime}}.

Note that, since
\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}
is abelian, each conjugacy class of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)
has the form

\mathcal{O}\times\{t\}
, where
\mathcal{O}
is a conjugacy class of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
t\in\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)
.

Proof.

(a) By
[
43
]
, since the Poisson algebraic group
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
is quasitriangular, its
double Poisson algebraic group is canonically isomorphic to

D(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})\simeq\big(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)\times\big(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big).

Theorem
7.8
(b) implies that the dual Poisson Lie group of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
is

\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\hookrightarrow\big(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)\times\big(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)

with the opposite Poisson structure to the restriction of the one of the double.
Both
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}\simeq M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
have the same tangent
Lie bialgebra, hence the map

\tau:=(\tau_{+}^{-1}\pi_{+},\tau_{-}^{-1}\pi_{-}):\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\simeq M_{\mathfrak{q}}

is a Poisson covering map. By the Semenov-Tian-Shansky dressing method
[
47
]
,
we get that the symplectic leaves of
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
are the connected components of the intersections

\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\cap\big(\operatorname{diag}(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})\cdot g\cdot\operatorname{diag}(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})\big),

where
\operatorname{diag}\big(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)
denotes the diagonal of
(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})^{\times 2}
and
g\in(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})^{\times 2}
.
Now we apply
[
52
, Theorem 1.10]
to obtain that each such intersection is a dense, open and connected subset of

\operatorname{diag}\big(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)\cdot g\cdot\operatorname{diag}\big(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)
.
Consider the map

\displaystyle\widetilde{j}:\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\to\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{j}(m_{+},m_{-}):=m_{+}^{-1}m_{-},

\displaystyle m_{+}\in\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant},m_{-}\in\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}.

By a direct argument we conclude that each symplectic leaf of
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
is of the form

\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}:=\big(\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\big)\cap\mathcal{O}^{\prime},

where
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
is a conjugacy class of
G_{\mathfrak{q}}\times T_{\mathfrak{q}}
, and that

\dim\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}=\dim\mathcal{O}^{\prime}.

Since
\tau:\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\times\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a covering of Poisson
Lie groups, each symplectic leaf of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}
is of the form
\tau(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}})
. One easily verifies that the diagram

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 19.19449pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.19449pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 43.49132pt\raise 6.61111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{j}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 81.39236pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.68787pt\raise-16.61278pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-25.89226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 81.39236pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 98.53822pt\raise-23.72557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 69.19449pt\raise-33.22557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.19449pt\raise-33.22557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

commutes, where
\psi:\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\big(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}\big)
is the canonical projection.
Clearly,
\psi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})=\mathcal{O}\times\{t\}
, where
\mathcal{O}
is a conjugacy class of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
t\in\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.
Therefore all symplectic leaves of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}
are of the form
\tau(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}})=j^{-1}\psi(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})=j^{-1}(\mathcal{O}\times\{t\})

and
\dim j^{-1}(\mathcal{O}\times\{t\})=\dim\mathcal{O}^{\prime}=\dim\mathcal{O}
.

Part (b) follows from part (a), and Theorems
2.3
and
6.2
.
∎

In regard to the irreducible representations of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
we wonder whether the De Concini–Kac–Procesi conjecture
could be extended to the setting of Theorem
8.2
, see
[
18
]
.

Question 8.3
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a conjugacy class of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
t\in\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{C}_{\mathfrak{q}}
and
z\in j^{-1}(\mathcal{O}\times\{t\})
. Does
\ell^{\dim\mathcal{O}/2}
divide
the dimension of any irreducible representation of
\mathcal{H}_{z}
?

8.2.
The torus orbits of symplectic leaves and the representations of the large quantum Borel algebras

The algebras
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
are

\mathbb{Z}^{\mathbb{I}}
-graded with grading
\deg e_{i}=-\deg f_{i}=\alpha_{i}
,
\deg K_{i}=\deg L_{i}=0
for
i\in\mathbb{I}
. This leads to a
canonical action of the torus
(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{\mathbb{I}}
on these algebras by algebra automorphisms, which preserves the
central subalgebras
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
,
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
and
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\pm}
.

By a direct comparison, one obtains that the
(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{\mathbb{I}}
-action on
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
corresponds to the left action of

\tau_{+}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{q}})
on
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
in the sense that every automorphism from the first one corresponds to
an automorphism from the second and vice versa. Similarly, the
(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{\mathbb{I}}
-action on
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
corresponds to the left action of

\tau_{-}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{q}})
on
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
. Theorem
8.2
(a) implies that the induced action of
(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{\mathbb{I}}
on

M_{\mathfrak{q}}
preserves the symplectic leaves of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}
. So, in regard to irreps of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
, the
(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{\mathbb{I}}
-automorphisms
of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}
do not provide any additional information to that in Theorem
8.2
(a).

However, for
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
, we do obtain additional representation theoretic information from the
(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{\mathbb{I}}
-action,
as stated in next theorem. Let
W_{\mathfrak{q}}
be the Weyl group of
G_{\mathfrak{q}}
, i.e., that of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Theorem 8.4
.

For every choice of the specialization parameters

t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
the following hold:

(a)

The Poisson structure on
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
is invariant under the left and right actions of
\tau^{-1}_{+}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
.
The
\tau^{-1}_{+}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
-orbits of symplectic leaves of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
are the double Bruhat cells

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}_{+}(\widetilde{B}^{+}_{\mathfrak{q}}\cap\widetilde{B}^{-}_{\mathfrak{q}}w\widetilde{B}^{-}_{\mathfrak{q}}),

\displaystyle w\in W_{\mathfrak{q}}.

(b)

If
\tau_{+}(z)
and
\tau_{+}(z^{\prime})
are in the same double Bruhat cell, then there is an algebra isomorphism

\mathcal{H}_{z}^{\geqslant}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{z^{\prime}}^{\geqslant}.

Proof.

(a) For a Lie subalgebra of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\oplus\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, denote by
N(-)
its normalizer in
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
. By
[
39
, Lemma 2.12]
,
the left and right actions of
\widetilde{M}_{\geqslant}\cap N((\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*})
on the Lie group
\widetilde{M}_{\geqslant}
preserve its Poisson structure.
By the definition of
\tau_{+}
, these actions correspond to the left and right actions of
\tau_{+}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{q}})
on
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
, so the latter
preserve the Poisson structure on
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
, because
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
is a Poisson map.

Applying
[
39
, Theorem 2.7 and Proposition 2.15]
and the Bruhat decomposition of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
,
we obtain that the
\widetilde{M}_{\geqslant}\cap N((\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant})^{*})
-orbits of symplectic leaves of

\widetilde{M}_{\geqslant}
(with respect to either action) are the intersections

\widetilde{M}_{\geqslant}\cap\big((\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})w(\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}\times\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})\big)

for
w\in W_{\mathfrak{q}}
. Since
\widetilde{M}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
is a Poisson covering map
and
\tau_{+}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
is an isomorphism (Theorem
7.10
),
the
\tau^{-1}_{+}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
-orbits of symplectic leaves of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
(with respect to either action) are the double Bruhat cells

\tau^{-1}_{+}(\widetilde{B}^{+}_{\mathfrak{q}}\cap\widetilde{B}^{-}_{\mathfrak{q}}w\widetilde{B}^{-}_{\mathfrak{q}})
for
w\in W_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Part (b) follows from part (a), Theorems
2.3
and
6.2
, and the fact that the left action of
\tau_{+}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
on

M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
comes from the
(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{\mathbb{I}}
-action on
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
by algebra automorphisms.
∎

Example 8.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}
be of type
\mathtt{wk}(4)
. By Example
7.11
, the corresponding algebraic group
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}
is isomorphic to

\operatorname{PSL}_{3}(\mathbb{C})\times\operatorname{PSL}_{3}(\mathbb{C})
whose Weyl group is
S_{3}\times S_{3}
.
Theorem
8.4
implies that among the quotients
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}/\mathfrak{M}^{\leqslant}_{z}U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}

for
z
in the maximal spectrum of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
, there are at most
|S_{3}\times S_{3}|=(3!)^{2}=36

isomorphism classes of finite dimensional algebras.

Analogously to Theorem
8.4
one proves the following:

Proposition 8.6
.

For every choice of the specialization parameters

t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
the following hold:

(a)

The Poisson structure on
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
is invariant under the left and right actions of
\tau^{-1}_{-}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
.
The
\tau^{-1}_{-}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
-orbits of symplectic leaves of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
are the double Bruhat cells

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}_{-}(\widetilde{B}^{-}_{\mathfrak{q}}\cap\widetilde{B}^{+}_{\mathfrak{q}}w\widetilde{B}^{+}_{\mathfrak{q}}),

\displaystyle w\in W_{\mathfrak{q}}.

(b)

If
\tau_{-}(z)
and
\tau_{-}(z^{\prime})
are in the same double Bruhat cell, then
\mathcal{H}_{z}^{\leqslant}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{z^{\prime}}^{\leqslant}
as algebras.

8.3.
Poisson homogeneous spaces and irreps of large quantum unipotent algebras

Since

Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}
is the algebra of coinvariants for the coaction of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0+}
on
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
obtained by restricting the coaction
of
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{0+}
on
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
, and analogously for the negative part, we have isomorphisms of Poisson algebras

(8.3)

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\simeq\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}/\tau_{+}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})],

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\simeq\mathbb{C}[M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}/\tau_{-}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})].

As shown in the previous subsection, the left and right actions of
\tau_{+}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
and
\tau_{-}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})

on the Poisson algebraic groups
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}
preserve their Poisson structures.
The right hand sides
of the isomorphisms (
8.3
) involve the coordinate rings of the resulting
Poisson homogeneous spaces
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}/\tau_{+}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}/\tau_{-}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})

obtained by taking quotients with respect to the right actions.
The Poisson structures on
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}/\tau_{+}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
and
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}/\tau_{-}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})

are invariant under the induced left actions of
\tau_{+}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
and
\tau_{-}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
.
By Theorem
7.10
,
\tau_{+}
restricts to the isomorphism of homogeneous spaces

\tau_{+}:M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}/j_{+}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}/\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}
. Denote the canonical isomorphism

\upsilon:\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}/\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}/\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\subset\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{q}}/\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}.

Theorem 8.7
.

For every choice of the specialization parameters

t_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying the Non-degeneracy Assumption
7.5
the following hold:

(a)

The
\tau^{-1}_{+}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
-orbits of symplectic leaves of
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\geqslant}/\tau_{+}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
are the open Richardson varieties

\displaystyle\tau^{-1}_{+}\upsilon^{-1}\big((\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\cap\widetilde{B}^{-}_{\mathfrak{q}}w\widetilde{B}^{-}_{\mathfrak{q}})/\widetilde{B}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}\big),

\displaystyle w\in W_{\mathfrak{q}}.

(b)

If
\upsilon\tau_{+}(z)
and
\upsilon\tau_{+}(z^{\prime})
are in the same open Richardson variety, then there is an isomorphism of algebras

\mathcal{H}_{z}^{+}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{z^{\prime}}^{+}.

Proof.

Part (a) is proved arguing as in the proof of Theorem
8.4
(a).
Then (b) is a consequence of (a), and
Theorems
2.3
and
6.2
.
∎

An analogous result holds for the large quantum unipotent algebra
U_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-}

and the torus orbits of symplectic leaves of the Poisson homogeneous space
M_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\leqslant}/\tau_{-}^{-1}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathfrak{q}})
.

Appendix A
Families of finite-dimensional Nichols algebras

Let
\theta\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbb{I}=\mathbb{I}_{\theta}
. We fix a matrix
\mathfrak{q}=(q_{ij})\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}

such that
\dim{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{q}}<\infty
. To insure centrality of
Z_{\mathfrak{q}}
we require

(a)
.

The matrix
\mathfrak{q}
satisfies (
4.27
), i.e.,

q_{\alpha_{i}\beta}^{N_{\beta}}=1
, for all
i\in\mathbb{I}
,
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Remark A.1
.

If the Dynkin diagram of
\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}
is as in Tables
1
,
2
and
3
, then there is
\mathfrak{q}
with the same Dynkin diagram that satisfies (
4.27
); the proof is straightforward.

If
\mathfrak{q}
satisfies (
4.27
), then
any matrix in its Weyl-equivalence class also does.
Let
\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1}]
be the algebra of Laurent polynomials; its group of units is

\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1}]^{\times}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\nu^{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let

(A.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{q}=(\mathbf{q}_{ij})\in\big(\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1}]^{\times}\big)^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}

For
x\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, we denote by
\mathbf{q}(x)

the matrix obtained by the evaluation
\operatorname{ev}:\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1}]\to\mathbb{C}
,
\operatorname{ev}(\nu)=x
.
We seek for matrices (
A.1
) with the following properties
(b)

and
(d)
.

(b)
.

The Nichols algebra of the
\mathbb{C}(\nu)
-braided vector space of diagonal type with braiding matrix (
A.1
) has the same arithmetic root system as
\mathfrak{q}
.

By inspection of the list in
[
30
]
–see also the exposition in
[
2
]
–we conclude that the only possible matrices
(
A.1
) are those Weyl-equivalent to the ones with Dynkin diagrams as
in Tables
1
,
2

and
3
and that the following property holds.

(c)
.

There exists an open subset
\emptyset\neq O\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{\times}

such that for any
x\in O
, the root systems and Weyl groupoids
associated to
\mathbf{q}
and
\mathbf{q}(x)
are isomorphic.
Also there exists
\xi\in\mathbb{G}_{\infty}^{\prime}\cap O

with
N:=\operatorname{ord}\xi\in[2,\infty)
such that
\mathfrak{q}=\mathbf{q}(\xi)
.

Remark A.2
.

(i). The Dynkin diagrams of the matrices
\mathbf{q}
and
\mathfrak{q}

locally have the form

\textstyle{\overset{\mathbf{q}_{ii}}{\underset{\ }{\circ}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{ij}}
\textstyle{\overset{\mathbf{q}_{jj}}{\underset{\ }{\circ}}}
,
respectively
\textstyle{\overset{q_{ii}}{\underset{\ }{\circ}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{q}_{ij}}
\textstyle{\overset{q_{jj}}{\underset{\ }{\circ}}}
,
where
\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{ij}=\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}
,
\widetilde{q}_{ij}=q_{ij}q_{ji}
,
i.e., the Dynkin diagram does not determine completely the braiding matrix. We deal with this as follows.
Let
\mathfrak{p}=(p_{ij})\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}
with the same Dynkin diagram as
\mathfrak{q}
. Then there exists

\mathbf{p}\in\big(\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1}]^{\times}\big)^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}
with the same Dynkin diagram as
\mathbf{q}
such that

\mathfrak{p}=\mathbf{p}(\xi)
. For, take
\mathbf{p}_{ii}=\mathbf{q}_{ii}
and
\mathbf{p}_{ij}\in\mathbb{C}[\nu^{\pm 1}]^{\times}
such that

p_{ij}=\mathbf{p}_{ij}(\xi)
for
i<j
;
then
\mathbf{p}_{ji}=\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{ij}\mathbf{p}_{ij}^{-1}
.

(ii). Assume that
\mathbf{q}
satisfies
(b)
.
Let
\mathbf{p}
be another matrix with the same diagram as (
A.1
).
Then
\mathbf{q}_{ij}=\mathbf{p}_{ij}\nu^{h_{ij}N}
,
i<j
for a unique family
(h_{ij})_{i<j\in\mathbb{I}}
with
h_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(d)
.

{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}
defined in (
6.2
) is invertible.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}
be the diagonal matrix with entries
N_{\beta}
,
\underline{\beta}\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}
.
The matrix
{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}
is invertible if and only if the auxiliary matrix
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbf{q}}
is so,
where

\displaystyle{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{q}}=-\xi^{-1}{\mathcal{N}}{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbf{q}}{\mathcal{N}}.

Proposition A.3
.

There exist matrices
C=(c_{ij})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}

and
(\mathtt{p}_{ij})\in(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}
such that
C
is symmetric and:

(i)

There are infinitely many matrices
T=(t_{ij})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}
fulfilling

(A.2)

\displaystyle t_{ii}

\displaystyle=c_{ii},

\displaystyle t_{ij}+t_{ji}

\displaystyle=c_{ij}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }i\neq j\in\mathbb{I}

such that the matrix
\mathbf{q}=(\mathbf{q}_{ij})
defined by

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{q}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{p}_{ij}\nu^{t_{ij}},

\displaystyle\text{ for all }i,j\in\mathbb{I}

satisfies
(b)
.

(ii)

Among those
T
in
(i)
, there infinitely many such that

\mathbf{q}
satisfies
(d)
.

Proof.

It suffices to fix one matrix for each Weyl-equivalence class, see Lemma
6.1
.
We check below
(i)
by case-by-case considerations computing also
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbf{q}}

and proving that it is invertible for infinitely many
T
.

A.1.
Cartan type

Let
\mathfrak{q}
be in this class; then there is a Cartan matrix
A=(a_{ij})_{i,j\in\mathbb{I}}

such that
q_{ij}q_{ji}=q_{ii}^{a_{ij}}
. We fix
d_{i}\in\mathbb{I}_{3}
such that

d_{i}a_{ij}=d_{j}a_{ji}
for all
i,j\in\mathbb{I}
.
The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{q}}
has the same type except when
N
is even and
A
is
of type
B_{\theta}
or
C_{\theta}
, when they are interchanged.
In this case
\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}=\{N_{i}\alpha_{i}:i\in\mathbb{I}\}
, so (
4.27
) becomes:

(A.4)

\displaystyle q_{ij}^{N_{j}}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\text{ for all }i,j\in\mathbb{I}.

The matrix
\mathbf{q}
we are looking for should also satisfy

\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}=\mathbf{q}_{ii}^{a_{ij}}
for all
i\neq j
.
In all cases we take
\xi=q_{11}
except for
B_{\theta}
, where
\xi=q_{\theta\theta}
;
see Table
1
. Set
t_{ii}=d_{i}
and
\mathbf{q}_{ii}=\nu^{t_{ii}}
.
Thus
\mathbf{q}_{ii}(\xi)=q_{ii}
for all
i\in\mathbb{I}
.
Recall that

\displaystyle(\nu-\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{j}}(\nu)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbf{q}_{ij}^{N_{i}N_{j}}.

For instance
\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}}(\nu)=\frac{1-\nu^{d_{i}N_{i}^{2}}}{\nu-\xi}
hence

(A.5)

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=-\xi^{-1}d_{i}N_{i}^{2}=-\xi^{-1}t_{ii}N_{i}^{2}.

Let
i<j
.
We see that there exists
d_{j}\in\mathbb{I}_{3}
such that
N_{j}=N/d_{j}
. By (
A.4
),

q_{ij}
is a power of
\xi^{d_{j}}
; choose
t_{ij}\in d_{j}\mathbb{Z}
such that
\mathbf{q}_{ij}=\nu^{t_{ij}}

satisfies
\mathbf{q}_{ij}(\xi)=\xi^{t_{ij}}=q_{ij}
.
Set
t_{ji}=d_{i}a_{ij}-t_{ij}
and
\mathbf{q}_{ji}=\nu^{t_{ji}}
.
We have defined
T
satisfying (
A.2
) and

\mathbf{q}
turns out to be given by (
A.3
) with
\mathtt{p}_{ij}=1
for all
i,j
,
i.e.,
(i)
holds. Also for all
i\neq j
,

(\nu-\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{j}}(\nu)=1-\nu^{t_{ij}N_{i}N_{j}}
and

(A.6)

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{j}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=-\xi^{-1}t_{ij}N_{i}N_{j}.

Therefore
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbf{q}}=T
. Observe that if
t_{ij}=0
for
i<j
, then

\det{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbf{q}}\neq 0
. By a standard argument,
(ii)
holds.

A.2.
Super type

Assume that the braiding matrix
\mathfrak{q}
is of super type; see
[
2
]
for details and below for
{\mathbf{D}}(2,1;\alpha)
.
Going over the list, we see that there exist

\circ

\xi\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, a root of 1 of order
N>1
;

\circ

a symmetric matrix
B=(b_{ij})_{i,j\in\mathbb{I}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{I}}
with

b_{ij}=1
for at least one pair
(i,j)
;

\circ

a parity vector
\mathtt{p}=(\mathtt{p}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{p}_{\theta})\in\{\pm 1\}^{\mathbb{I}}

with
\mathtt{p}_{i}=-1
when
b_{ii}=0
; such that

\displaystyle q_{ij}q_{ji}

\displaystyle=\xi^{b_{ij}},

\displaystyle i

\displaystyle\neq j;

\displaystyle q_{ii}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{p}_{i}\xi^{b_{ii}},

\displaystyle i

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{I}.

We describe in Table
2
matrices
\mathbf{q}
of super type, one for each
Weyl-equivalence class (here
\alpha_{(ij)}:=\alpha_{i}+\dots+\alpha_{j}
for
i<j
).
Since the matrix
\mathfrak{q}
has an analogous shape, we may assume that

\circ

there exists
k\in\mathbb{I}
such that
\{i\in\mathbb{I}:\mathtt{p}_{i}=-1\}=\{k\}
;

\circ

there exists
h\in\mathbb{I}
,
h\neq k
, such that
\xi=q_{hh}
.

Recall that
\widetilde{\varPi}
was defined in (
6.4
) and (
6.7
). Therefore we have:

\circ

either
\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}=\{N_{i}\alpha_{i}:i\in\mathbb{I},i\neq k\}\cup\{\mathtt{N}\alpha_{k}\}
(
\mathtt{N}=N
if
N
is even and
\mathtt{N}=2N
if
N
is odd) for type
{\mathbf{A}}(k-1|\theta-k)

or else there exists a unique positive non-simple root
\beta
such that

\widetilde{\varPi}^{\mathfrak{q}}=\{N_{i}\alpha_{i}:i\in\mathbb{I},i\neq k\}\cup\{N_{\beta}\beta\}
;

\circ

for
i\in\mathbb{I}
,
i\neq k
, we may (and do) choose
b_{ii}\in\{\pm 1,\pm 2,\pm 3\}
.
Then
N_{i}=LCD(b_{ii},N)
; set
d_{i}=N/N_{i}
.

We start defining the matrix
\mathbf{q}
.
First we take
t_{ii}=b_{ii}
and
\mathbf{q}_{ii}=\mathtt{p}_{i}q^{b_{ii}}
for all
i\in\mathbb{I}
.

Condition (
4.27
) says that
q_{ij}^{N_{j}}=1
, for all
i\in\mathbb{I}
,

j\in\mathbb{I}\backslash\{k\}
.
Let
i<j
with
j\neq k
; choose
t_{ij}\in d_{j}\mathbb{Z}
and set

t_{ji}=b_{ji}-t_{ij}
.
Then
\mathbf{q}_{ij}=\nu^{t_{ij}}
and
\mathbf{q}_{ji}=\nu^{t_{ji}}

satisfy
\mathbf{q}_{ij}(\xi)=\xi^{t_{ij}}=q_{ij}
and
\mathbf{q}_{ij}\mathbf{q}_{ji}=\nu^{b_{ij}}
.

Similarly,
q_{ki}^{N_{i}}=1
for
k>i
, so choose
t_{ik}\in d_{i}\mathbb{Z}
and set
t_{ki}=b_{ki}-t_{ik}
,

\mathbf{q}_{ki}=\nu^{b_{ki}-t_{ik}}
and
\mathbf{q}_{ik}=\nu^{t_{ik}}
so that
\mathbf{q}_{ki}(\xi)=\xi^{t_{ki}}=q_{ki}
.
We have defined
T
satisfying (
A.2
) and

\mathbf{q}
turns out to be given by (
A.3
) with
\mathtt{p}_{ii}=\mathtt{p}_{i}
and
\mathtt{p}_{ij}=1
for all
i\neq j
,
i.e.,
(i)
holds.

It remains to compute the matrix
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbf{q}}
. Arguing as in the Cartan case we see that

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=-\xi^{-1}\mathtt{p}_{i}b_{ii}N_{i}^{2},

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\alpha_{j}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=-\xi^{-1}t_{ij}N_{i}N_{j},

\displaystyle i,j

\displaystyle\in\mathbb{I}\backslash\{k\}.

Assume that there exists
\gamma\in\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}\backslash\mathbb{I}
(a non-simple Cartan root).
Then there exist
\mathtt{p}_{\gamma}\in\{\pm 1\}
and
b_{\gamma\gamma},b_{i\gamma}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{q}_{\gamma\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{p}_{\gamma}\nu^{b_{\gamma\gamma}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{q}_{i\gamma}\mathbf{q}_{\gamma i}

\displaystyle=\nu^{b_{i\gamma}}.

Extend
(t_{ij})
to a bilinear form
t:\mathbb{Z}^{\mathbb{I}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\mathbb{I}}\to\mathbb{Z}
.
Then for
k\neq i\in\mathbb{I}
,

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\gamma}(\xi)

\displaystyle=-\xi^{-1}\mathtt{p}_{\gamma}b_{\gamma\gamma}N_{\gamma}^{2},

\displaystyle\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\alpha_{i}\gamma}(\xi)

\displaystyle=-\xi^{-1}t_{i\gamma}N_{i}N_{\gamma},

\displaystyle(\nu-\xi)\wp^{\mathbf{q}}_{\gamma\alpha_{i}}(\nu)

\displaystyle=-\xi^{-1}t_{\gamma i}N_{i}N_{\gamma}.

All in all,

{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbf{q}}
is of the form
\left(t_{\alpha\beta}\right)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}\times\varPi^{\mathfrak{q}}}
,
where
t_{\alpha\alpha}=\mathtt{p}_{\alpha}b_{\alpha\alpha}
,

t_{r\alpha\beta}+t_{\beta\alpha}=b_{\alpha\beta}
for
\alpha\neq\beta
.
Arguing as in the Cartan case, we conclude that
(ii)
holds.

.

).

; we choose

minimal such that

,

.

such that
\mathfrak{q}=\mathbf{q}(\xi)
.

Appendix B
Lie bialgebras and Poisson algebraic groups

We gather minimal background material on Lie bialgebras and Poisson algebraic groups
for Sections
7
and
8
. We refer to
[
24
, Section 2-7]
for a full treatment.

B.1.
Lie bialgebras

Recall that a Lie bialgebra is a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
equipped with a linear map
\delta:\mathfrak{g}\to\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}
such that

(i)

the dual of the map
\delta
defines a Lie algebra structure of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
and

(ii)

\delta
is a 1-cocycle, i.e.,
\delta([a,b])=\operatorname{ad}_{a}(\delta(b))-\operatorname{ad}_{b}(\delta(a))
for all
a,b\in\mathfrak{g}
.

The Lie bialgebras with opposite cobracket (same bracket) and opposite bracket (same cobracket)
will be denoted by
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{op}}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{op}}
, respectively.
The dual Lie bialgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
of
\mathfrak{g}
is the Lie bialgebra with Lie bracket and cobracket given by

\displaystyle\langle[f,g],a\rangle=\langle f\otimes g,\delta(a)\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle\delta(f),a\otimes b\rangle=\langle f,[a,b]\rangle,

\displaystyle\forall a,b\in\mathfrak{g},f,g\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}.

The Drinfeld double
D(\mathfrak{g})
of the Lie bialgebra
\mathfrak{g}
is a Lie bialgebra which is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}^{*}
as a vector space and
is uniquely defined by the conditions:

(a)

The canonical embeddings
\iota:\mathfrak{g}\hookrightarrow D(\mathfrak{g})
and
\iota^{*}:(\mathfrak{g}^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}\hookrightarrow D(\mathfrak{g})
are embeddings of Lie bialgebras;

(b)

For
a\in\mathfrak{g}\subset D(\mathfrak{g})
,
f\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\subset D(\mathfrak{g})
,
[x,f]=\operatorname{ad}_{x}^{*}(f)-\operatorname{ad}_{f}^{*}(x)
in terms of the coadjoint actions of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

A quadratic Lie algebra is a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
equipped with an non-degenerate invariant symmetric bilinear form
(.,.)
. A Manin triple is a triple
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}_{+},\mathfrak{g}_{-})

consisting of a quadratic Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},(.,.))
and a pair of isotropic Lie subalgebras
\mathfrak{g}_{\pm}\subset\mathfrak{g}
.

Remark B.1
.

The notions of Drinfeld double and Manin triple are equivalent in the case of finite dimensional Lie algebras:

(a)

Each Drinfeld double
D(\mathfrak{g})
is a quadratic Lie algebra with symmetric bilinear form

\displaystyle(a+f,b+g)=\langle f,b\rangle+\langle g,a\rangle,

\displaystyle a,b\in\mathfrak{g},f,g\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}.

With respect to this form,
(D(\mathfrak{g}),\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{*})
is a Manin triple.

(b)

For a Manin triple
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}_{+},\mathfrak{g}_{-})
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\pm}
have canonical Lie bialgebra structures given by

\displaystyle(\delta(a),f\otimes g)=(a,[f,g]),

\displaystyle(\delta(f),a\otimes b)=-(f,[a,b]),

\displaystyle\forall a,b\in\mathfrak{g}_{+},f,g\in\mathfrak{g}_{-}.

Then
\mathfrak{g}
, equipped with the Lie cobracket
\delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{+}}+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{-}}
, is isomorphic to the Drinfeld double of
\mathfrak{g}_{+}
,
and
\mathfrak{g}_{-}\simeq(\mathfrak{g}_{+}^{*})^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Here is an important class of Lie bialgebras:

(\mathfrak{g},\delta)
is
quasitriangular
if
\delta(x)=\operatorname{ad}x(r)
for all
x\in\mathfrak{g}

where
r=\sum_{i}r_{i}\otimes r^{i}\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}

satisfies the classical Yang-Baxter equation:

[r^{12},r^{13}]+[r^{12},r^{23}]+[r^{13},r^{23}]=0;

here
r^{12}=r\otimes 1
,
r^{13}=\sum_{i}r_{i}\otimes 1\otimes r^{i}
,
r^{23}=1\otimes r
. In this case we set

(\mathfrak{g},r):=(\mathfrak{g},\delta)
. The Drinfled double is the archetypical example of a quasitriangular Lie bialgebra.

Let
r^{21}=\sum_{i}r^{i}\otimes r_{i}
.
Recall that a quasitriangular Lie bialgebra
(\mathfrak{g},r)
is called

factorizable
if
r+r^{21}\in S^{2}\mathfrak{g}

defines a nondegenerate inner product on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}

[
43
]
.

B.2.
Poisson algebraic groups

A (complex) Poisson algebraic group is an algebraic group
G
equipped with
a bivector field
\pi
such that the product map

(G,\pi)\times(G,\pi)\to(G,\pi)

is Poisson. The coordinate ring
\mathbb{C}[G]
has a canonical structure of
commutative Poisson-Hopf algebra with Poisson bracket given by

\displaystyle\{f,g\}:=\langle df\otimes dg,\pi\rangle,

\displaystyle f,g\in\mathbb{C}[G],

where
df
denotes the differential of
f
. Conversely, every finitely generated commutative
Poisson-Hopf algebra
H
gives rise to the Poisson algebraic group
\operatorname{MaxSpec}H
.

The tangent Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}=T_{1}G
of every Poisson algebraic group
G
has a canonical Lie bialgebra structure.
The Poisson structure
\pi
automatically vanishes at the identity element
1
of
G
.
The Lie cobracket on
\mathfrak{g}
, or equivalently the Lie bracket on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\simeq T_{1}^{*}G
, is defined
as the linearization of
\pi
at
1
:

(B.1)

\displaystyle[d_{1}(f),d_{1}(g)]:=d_{1}\big(\{f,g\}\big),

\displaystyle f,g\in\mathbb{C}[G].

In Hopf algebra situations it is advantageous to describe the tangent Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of an algebraic group
G
by describing the corresponding Lie cobracket
on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}=T_{1}^{*}G
.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
G
be a complex algebraic group; as usual

\Delta(f)=f_{(1)}\otimes f_{(2)}
for
f\in\mathbb{C}[G]
. Then the canonical Lie coalgebra structure on

T_{1}^{*}G\simeq\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is given by

\displaystyle\delta(d_{1}f)=d_{1}f_{(1)}\wedge d_{1}f_{(2)},

\displaystyle f\in\mathbb{C}[G].

B.3.
The classification of Belavin and Drinfeld

We fix a complex finite-dimensional simple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. Pick a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
and
a set
\Delta\subset\mathfrak{h}^{*}
of simple roots.
The Casimir element
\Omega\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
of
\mathfrak{g}
is the symmetric tensor
associated to the Killing form of
\mathfrak{g}
; the component of
\Omega
in
\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}

is denoted by
\Omega_{0}
.

Definition B.3
.

A Belavin-Drinfeld triple (BD-triple for short) is a triple

(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},T)
where
\Gamma_{1},

\Gamma_{2}
are subsets of

\Delta
and

T:\Gamma_{1}\to\Gamma_{2}
is a bijection that preserves the inner
product
and satisfies the nilpotency condition:
for any
\alpha\in\Gamma_{1}
there exists a positive integer
n
for
which

T^{n}(\alpha)
belongs to
\Gamma_{2}
but not to
\Gamma_{1}
.

Given a BD-triple
(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},T)
, we denote by

\widehat{\Gamma_{i}}
the set of positive roots
lying in the subgroup generated by
\Gamma_{i}
, for
i=1,2
.
There is an associated partial ordering on
\Phi^{+}
given by

\alpha\prec\beta
if

\alpha\in\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}
,

\beta\in\widehat{\Gamma_{2}}
, and
\beta=T^{n}(\alpha)

for a positive integer
n
.

A
continuous parameter
for the BD-triple

(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},T)

is an element
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{\otimes 2}
such that

(B.2)

\displaystyle(T(\alpha)\otimes 1)\lambda+(1\otimes\alpha)\lambda

\displaystyle=0,\quad\text{for all }\alpha\in\Gamma_{1},

(B.3)

\displaystyle\lambda+\lambda^{21}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{0}.

Let
\mathfrak{a}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{a}_{2}
be the reductive subalgebras of
\mathfrak{g}
with Cartan
subalgebras
generated by
h_{\alpha}
,
\alpha
in
\Gamma_{1}
, resp. in
\Gamma_{2}
,
and
with Dynkin diagrams
\Gamma_{1}
, respectively
\Gamma_{2}
. We extend
T
to
a Lie algebra isomorphism
\widehat{T}:\mathfrak{a}_{1}\to\mathfrak{a}_{2}
.

Theorem B.4
.

[
11
]
.
Let
(\mathfrak{g},r)
be a factorizable Lie bialgebra with underlying simple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
.
Then there exist a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
,
a set of simple roots
\Delta
, a BD-triple
(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},T)
, a continuous parameter
\lambda

and
t\in\mathbb{C}-0
such that the
r
is given by

(B.4)

r=t\Big(\lambda+\sum_{\alpha\in\Phi^{+}}x_{-\alpha}\otimes x_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha,\beta\in\Phi^{+},\alpha\prec\beta}x_{-\alpha}\wedge x_{\beta}\Big),

where
x_{\beta}\in\mathfrak{g}_{\beta}
,
\beta\in\pm\Phi^{+}
, are root vectors
normalized by

(B.5)

\displaystyle(x_{\beta}|x_{-\beta})

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\text{for all }\beta

\displaystyle\in\Phi^{+},

(B.6)

\displaystyle\widehat{T}(x_{\beta})

\displaystyle=x_{T(\beta)},

\displaystyle\text{for all }\beta

\displaystyle\in\Gamma_{1}.

Reciprocally the matrix
r
defined by (
B.4
) satisfies the classical Yang-Baxter equation, hence defines
a factorizable Lie bialgebra structure on
\mathfrak{g}
.
∎

Example B.5
.

We say that a BD-triple
(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},T)
is
empty
if

\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{2}=\emptyset
.
In this case any
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{\otimes 2}
is a continuous parameter;
the choice
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}(\sum_{i}h_{i}\otimes h_{i})
, for an orthonormal basis
h_{i}
of
\mathfrak{h}
, gives rise to the
standard
Poisson structure.

Appendix C
Symplectic cores and symplectic leaves

Let
Z
be an affine commutative Poisson algebra and
M\coloneqq\operatorname{MaxSpec}Z
.
As usual the point
x\in M
corresponds to the ideal
\mathfrak{M}_{x}
. The material below
is extracted from
[
13
]
.

The largest Poisson ideal contained in an ideal
I
of
Z
is called the

Poisson core
of
I
and denoted
\mathfrak{P}(I)
; it exists because the sum of
Poisson ideals is again a Poisson ideal.
If
I
is prime, then so is
\mathfrak{P}(I)
.
If
\mathfrak{M}
is maximal, then we say that
\mathfrak{P}(\mathfrak{M})
is Poisson primitive.
Every prime Poisson ideal of
Z
is an intersection of Poisson primitive ideals.

Definition C.1
.

A
symplectic core
is a class of the equivalence relation
\sim
given by

\displaystyle x\sim y

\displaystyle\iff\mathfrak{P}(\mathfrak{M}_{x})=\mathfrak{P}(\mathfrak{M}_{y}),

\displaystyle x,y

\displaystyle\in M.

The equivalence class of
x\in M
is denoted by
\mathscr{C}(x)
and called the symplectic core of
x
.
Any symplectic core is locally closed and smooth in its closure
[
13
, 3.3]
.

Assume for simplicity that
Z
is regular, i.e.,
M
is smooth, see
[
13
, 3.5]
for the general case.
Then
M
becomes a complex analytic Poisson manifold. Given
x\in M
, the
symplectic leaf

\mathscr{L}(x)

is the maximal connected complex analytic submanifold of
M

such that
x\in\mathscr{L}(x)
and the restriction of the Poisson bracket to
\mathscr{L}(x)

is nondegenerate at every point.
Concretely, the symplectic leaf
\mathscr{L}(x)
is formed by the points which can be reached from
x

by a piecewise smooth curve, each segment of which is a trajectory of a hamiltonian vector field.
Symplectic leaves might be not algebraic but they determine the symplectic cores.
Below the closure is relative to the Zariski topology.

Theorem C.2
.

[
27
, Th. 7.4]
Let
\mathscr{L}
be a symplectic leaf.
There is a unique symplectic core
\mathscr{C}
in
M
with
\mathscr{L}\subset\mathscr{C}\subseteq\overline{\mathscr{L}}
and

\mathscr{C}
is the unique symplectic core dense in
\overline{\mathscr{L}}
.
In fact

(C.1)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}=\overline{\mathscr{L}}\ \backslash\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathscr{K}\text{ symplectic leaf}\\
\overline{\mathscr{K}}\subsetneq\overline{\mathscr{L}}\end{subarray}}\overline{\mathscr{K}}.

Each symplectic core
\mathscr{C}
in
M
can be obtained as in (
C.1
).
